# AMA-L2 — Multi-Agent Systems

> **Current scientific edition — 13 November 2026.** Read the [substantive current research](current/AMA-L2.md) for the public scientific account, corrected predicates, guards, contrary findings and evidence limits. The record below is the earlier documentary cutoff. Its generic PENDING/private/no-public-body statements are dated history and do not describe the current edition. Historical scientific bodies and their original source identities remain unchanged.

## Two scientific layers

This reader covers **76 historical public Multi-Agent Systems rows**, frozen as `EMAS-R1`. The later accepted AMA-L2 subject also selects 76 identities; its subject SHA-256 is `791620825f2a382e291f05befea096a5eda481f0b9d7b99dc5f7363f7784c0ed`. Its later corrections, accepted independent reviews and unresolved scientific observations belong to that exact subject, not to a relabelled September record. [SOURCE_MAP.json](SOURCE_MAP.json) pins the separate subjects and receipts. The complete current interpretation is retained privately. `AMA-DISCLOSE-AMA-L2` remains **PENDING** for its detailed public release.

## Interaction changes the object being judged

Multi-Agent Systems concerns agents whose information, capabilities, control, incentives, commitments or outcomes affect one another. Several named workers do not by themselves establish a useful collective. EMAS-001's decomposition question asks whether an actual interdependence justifies the additional boundaries and coordination cost. Cooperative teams, strategic exchange, public commitments, distributed optimization, organization, argumentation and adaptive collectives are distinct mechanisms with different premises. [Historical intake][intake] · [Historical ledger][ledger]

The genealogy is plural. Contract Net connects tasks to bidders and contractors; Partial Global Planning coordinates interacting partial plans; joint-intention and SharedPlans accounts explain cooperative persistence and missing contributions; STEAM combines selected teamwork ideas. Public commitment approaches make obligations assessable among parties whose private mental states cannot be inspected. Economic and social-choice results contribute constraints on particular strategic models. Distributed constraints, argumentation and multi-agent learning address still different questions. Direct documentary transmission, shared ancestry, criticism and analogy are separate relation types in the historical genealogy. [Historical coverage][coverage]

A key predicate distinguishes **legal messaging from actual fulfillment**. A proposal, award, accepted obligation and completed effect need not be the same event. EMAS-010–012 concern conversation causality, information prerequisites and protocol meaning; EMAS-025/026 concern public commitment and valid transitions; EMAS-069/071 concern the collective effect and its evidential closure. A protocol can permit a completion path while participants indefinitely wait. A public promise can remain live when one participant abandons its local plan. An ordinary direct interaction with a sufficiently clear result can satisfy a bounded dependency without a general negotiation or institution. [EMAS-010–016, 025–027, 061, 069, 071][ledger]

Authority, knowledge and capability remain different facts. A role chart does not grant access to an external resource. A norm can be represented without being enforceable. Preventing a prohibited transition, detecting/sanctioning a completed violation and choosing voluntary compliance have different mechanisms. An equilibrium need not be socially desirable; a truthful-reporting result depends on its model and feasible implementation. [EMAS-003–004, 030–042][ledger]

Collective computation also retains its exact object. Constraint satisfaction is not optimization; a distributed optimizer's message count is not its complete memory or transmission cost; argument acceptance is not premise truth. Centralized training with decentralized execution requires a particular information relationship. A restricted monotonic value factorization does not represent every arbitrary joint payoff. A replicated solver remains subject to its actual fault and knowledge premises and the approximation status of its underlying algorithm. [EMAS-043–054, 075][ledger]

## Evidence, criticism and failure

Many agents repeating one unverified input do not supply independent observations. Self-play success need not transfer to new partners. Agreement can reflect shared bias, and an individually improved policy can worsen a collective resource outcome. Teamwork and explanation can impose costs without demonstrated benefits under a given task and measurement design. The historical corpus retains positive, negative and null evidence with their source-specific limits. Its 56 bibliography records are not 56 independent experiments. [EMAS-006, 048–050, 055–058, 064–067][ledger] · [Source table][sources]

The original EMAS-013 current-FIPA question is unresolved. Historical standards-family records and legal protocol models do not establish a selected runtime's current conformance or interoperability. A publication, deployment or repeated result likewise does not automatically establish comparative causal benefit. A later accepted scientific correction must remain attached to its own source version; the historical rows below are transcribed as history. [EMAS-013 and historical intake][intake]

## Guarded composition and alternatives

The public predecessor offers six configuration branches: direct coordination, revisable cooperative teamwork, strategic public interaction, distributed optimization, controlled adaptive collectives, and a human/institutional overlay. A centralized or direct comparator is admissible when it actually has the required information and powers. It cannot be given powers absent from the real setting merely to make it seem cheaper. The branches remain alternatives, not a mandatory all-of architecture. [Historical composition][composition]

Four inherited derived rows sharpen the composition: EMAS-071 connects observation, obligation, action and consequence; EMAS-072 governs handoff between assumption regimes; EMAS-073 selects minimum adequate coordination; EMAS-074 checks dependency effects of adaptation. These are analytical criteria with explicit parents and limits. They do not prove the net benefit of a complete deployed collective. More messaging, more members or more formalism is not the acceptance test. [EMAS-071–074][ledger]

## Separate native responsibility

Published, canonically unallocated **R0044 / #231** preserves the earlier complete 76-member documentary disposition and bounded comparison, including conditional, rejected, research-only and nontriggered outcomes. Its total-cost comparison is inconclusive where the public record says so; this package does not promote that result to broad MAS effectiveness. [Existing native qualification][native]

The AMA-L2 rough responsibility asks how a particular current inter-agent dependency is enacted, who remains responsible and which evidence actually reaches the parent or final consumer. R0036/#210 owns the scoped lane/normalization/parent-custody contract; R0035/#209 owns the required joins and single writer; R0033/#207 and R0037/#211 retain governor and lifecycle boundaries. Any later bounded work order belongs under the real existing owner after current integration and approval gates. [Owner map](OWNER_MAP.json) · [Four rough responsibilities](RLGWO_OUTLINES.md)

<a id="emas-001"></a>
### EMAS-001 — Interdependence-justified agent decomposition

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S001; EMAS-S004; EMAS-S045; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S001: Intelligent Agents: Theory and Practice (1995-06); EMAS-S004: The Contract Net Protocol: High-Level Communication and Control in a Distributed Problem Solver (1980-12); EMAS-S045: An Implementation of the Contract Net Protocol Based on Marginal Cost Calculations (1993); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** From loosely coupled distributed problem solving to cost-aware granularity and conditional multi-agent use.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Choose decision boundaries around genuinely separate capabilities, information, incentives or control; compare candidate decompositions against the same collective work.

**Problem:** Distributed ownership, unavailable central information or genuine concurrent capability can change achievable outcomes.

**Mechanism:** Choose decision boundaries around genuinely separate capabilities, information, incentives or control; compare candidate decompositions against the same collective work.

**Trigger and context:** Distributed ownership, unavailable central information or genuine concurrent capability can change achievable outcomes.

**Preconditions:** A defined task, explicit rights and comparable information/cost accounting.

**Decision or consumer:** System designer and service recipient

**Authority boundary:** Architects propose boundaries; operational principals approve rights and resource allocation.

**Communication or interaction:** Specify which dependencies require messages and whether a fixed direct interface suffices; natural language is optional. Specific coupling: Choose decision boundaries around genuinely separate capabilities, information, incentives or control; compare candidate decompositions against the same collective work.

**Observable criterion:** A stated dependency is addressed without a worse justified trade-off than the selected comparator.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** One agent, direct authorised allocation or ordinary services when they meet the same requirements at lower total cost.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Artificially fine-grained workers add negotiation and translation costs; a biased baseline manufactures benefit.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S006; EMAS-S037; EMAS-S041; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S006; LIMITS: Distributed vehicle-monitoring simulation, four-node difficult scenario; original meta-organisation is specified in advance. No numerical effect extracted from its results table. Simpler situations can lose from coordination overhead.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S037; LIMITS: FRAMES and MuSiQue 4-hop with selected model families are not all tasks. Bootstrap uncertainty and imperfect token controls matter. Researcher audit: Fano lower bounds alone do not order actual risks, and strict mutual-information loss need not strictly increase classification error. The broad title is not accepted as a universal theorem.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S041; LIMITS: Rule-based kitchen agent simulation, not a production LLM or human organisation study. Three-page report lacks a full replication protocol; communication restrictions can sometimes reduce redundant coalition formation.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Artificially fine-grained workers add negotiation and translation costs; a biased baseline manufactures benefit.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.

**Contrary evidence:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S006; EMAS-S037; EMAS-S041; ADJUDICATION: Artificially fine-grained workers add negotiation and translation costs; a biased baseline manufactures benefit.; NOT_A_REFUTATION_OF: A bounded theorem whose actual assumptions remain satisfied.

**Assumption sensitivity:** MATERIAL_ASSUMPTIONS: A defined task, explicit rights and comparable information/cost accounting.; CHANGE_THAT_REOPENS_JUDGEMENT: Which hidden ownership or locality constraint makes the simpler comparator infeasible?

**Unresolved questions:** Which hidden ownership or locality constraint makes the simpler comparator infeasible?

**Property versus ceremony:** MINIMAL_ADEQUATE_FORM: One agent, direct authorised allocation or ordinary services when they meet the same requirements at lower total cost.; ACTUAL_CONSUMER: System designer and service recipient; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT: Omit or retire when the stated trigger/dependency is absent and the observable postcondition is already secured by a cheaper adequate arrangement; preserve outstanding obligations during any transition.; FALSE_SUBSTITUTE: A representation or label without the stated postcondition and consumer does not establish this candidate.

### EMAS-002 — Explicit population and environment boundary

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S001; EMAS-S023; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S001: Intelligent Agents: Theory and Practice (1995-06); EMAS-S023: Multiagent Systems: Algorithmic, Game-Theoretic, and Logical Foundations (2009; manuscript revision also ©2010); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Plural agency accounts matured into model-specific population contracts rather than a universal anthropomorphic agent.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Identify participants, admission assumptions, external actors and environment effects before stating a collective property.

**Problem:** Any guarantee depends on who can act or supply information.

**Mechanism:** Identify participants, admission assumptions, external actors and environment effects before stating a collective property.

**Trigger and context:** Any guarantee depends on who can act or supply information.

**Preconditions:** Identifiable decision participants and external dependencies.

**Decision or consumer:** Analyst, evaluator and participant

**Authority boundary:** Modeller describes the boundary; it does not control excluded actors.

**Communication or interaction:** Specify which dependencies require messages and whether a fixed direct interface suffices; natural language is optional. Specific coupling: Identify participants, admission assumptions, external actors and environment effects before stating a collective property.

**Observable criterion:** The claim identifies included/excluded actors and changes that invalidate its assumptions.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A short boundary statement is sufficient for a fixed two-party interaction; no universal agent directory is required.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Unmodelled entrants or externally controlled services defeat a closed-population claim.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S040; EMAS-S051; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S040; LIMITS: Two-player discretised pricing simulation. Tabular pretraining: 500 games/1,000 policies; LLM comparison selects six GPT5-mini meta-strategies, 40 base-game samples, 50 rounds, four prices. Known cost types in meta-analysis and restricted strategy set limit transfer.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S051; LIMITS: At most k faulty replicas per relevant set and at least 2k+1 knowledge/replica coverage are material; communication multiplication is O(k²) per original message. Main paper inspected; supplementary impossibility proof not independently checked. Synthetic experiments do not certify arbitrary Byzantine MAS.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Unmodelled entrants or externally controlled services defeat a closed-population claim.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.

**Contrary evidence:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S040; EMAS-S051; ADJUDICATION: Unmodelled entrants or externally controlled services defeat a closed-population claim.; NOT_A_REFUTATION_OF: A bounded theorem whose actual assumptions remain satisfied.

**Assumption sensitivity:** MATERIAL_ASSUMPTIONS: Identifiable decision participants and external dependencies.; CHANGE_THAT_REOPENS_JUDGEMENT: Can material influences enter through an unlisted tool, model or person?

**Unresolved questions:** Can material influences enter through an unlisted tool, model or person?

**Property versus ceremony:** MINIMAL_ADEQUATE_FORM: A short boundary statement is sufficient for a fixed two-party interaction; no universal agent directory is required.; ACTUAL_CONSUMER: Analyst, evaluator and participant; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT: Omit or retire when the stated trigger/dependency is absent and the observable postcondition is already secured by a cheaper adequate arrangement; preserve outstanding obligations during any transition.; FALSE_SUBSTITUTE: A representation or label without the stated postcondition and consumer does not establish this candidate.

### EMAS-003 — Explicit goal alignment and incentive regime

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S023; EMAS-S046; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S023: Multiagent Systems: Algorithmic, Game-Theoretic, and Logical Foundations (2009; manuscript revision also ©2010); EMAS-S046: Issues in Automated Negotiation and Electronic Commerce: Extending the Contract Net Framework (1995); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Cooperative DAI and self-interested negotiation remain alternatives; one is not an automatic refinement of the other.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Classify shared-goal, competitive and mixed-motive interactions and state which utilities, obligations or preferences guide each decision.

**Problem:** One participant may benefit by deviating from collective instructions.

**Mechanism:** Classify shared-goal, competitive and mixed-motive interactions and state which utilities, obligations or preferences guide each decision.

**Trigger and context:** One participant may benefit by deviating from collective instructions.

**Preconditions:** Observable incentives or defensible behavioural assumptions, including outside options.

**Decision or consumer:** Mechanism selector and team organiser

**Authority boundary:** Participants retain objectives; analysts cannot author them into agreement.

**Communication or interaction:** Specify which dependencies require messages and whether a fixed direct interface suffices; natural language is optional. Specific coupling: Classify shared-goal, competitive and mixed-motive interactions and state which utilities, obligations or preferences guide each decision.

**Observable criterion:** No cooperative guarantee is applied to a strategic participant without a stated justification.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Declared common-purpose behaviour may suffice in a controlled team; economic mechanism design is not mandatory.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Nominal shared prompts conceal private rewards, incentives or unavailable sanctions.

### EMAS-004 — Separate information, capability, control and authority

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S018; EMAS-S020; EMAS-S022; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S018: The Gaia Methodology for Agent-Oriented Analysis and Design (2000); EMAS-S020: Normative Multi-Agent Programs and Their Logics (2008); EMAS-S022: Multi-Agent Systems: Technical & Ethical Challenges of Functioning in a Mixed Group (2022); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Role/permission models and normative artefacts expose the gap between computational and external institutions.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Distinguish knowing a fact, being technically able to act, being entitled to act and obtaining the intended consequence.

**Problem:** Allocation, institutional rules or human-agent delegation affect real resources.

**Mechanism:** Distinguish knowing a fact, being technically able to act, being entitled to act and obtaining the intended consequence.

**Trigger and context:** Allocation, institutional rules or human-agent delegation affect real resources.

**Preconditions:** Named action/resource and an identifiable source of authority.

**Decision or consumer:** Authorising principal and affected participant

**Authority boundary:** External principals define legitimate authority; the model only represents it.

**Communication or interaction:** Specify which dependencies require messages and whether a fixed direct interface suffices; natural language is optional. Specific coupling: Distinguish knowing a fact, being technically able to act, being entitled to act and obtaining the intended consequence.

**Observable criterion:** Every consequential action has both a capable actor and a valid decision boundary; observation alone is not treated as permission.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** An explicit direct permission can be sufficient; no role hierarchy is required for its own sake.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Powerful tools bypass the intended decision maker; formal norms are mistaken for legal authority.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** No such unconditional mechanism is retained; replace the claim with a threat-specific account of dependencies, replication and recovery.

**Problem:** The slogan is often invoked when a system has multiple processes or role names.

**Mechanism:** No such unconditional mechanism is retained; replace the claim with a threat-specific account of dependencies, replication and recovery.

**Trigger and context:** The slogan is often invoked when a system has multiple processes or role names.

<a id="emas-009"></a>
### EMAS-009 — Private mental-state sincerity as a universal compliance test

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S007; EMAS-S014; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S007: On Acting Together (1990); EMAS-S014: FIPA Communicative Act Library Specification (2001-08-10); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Development:** Preserved as a bounded semantic branch rather than accepted as a universal interoperability foundation.

### EMAS-010 — Conversation identity and information causality

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S012; EMAS-S016; EMAS-S044; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S012: KQML—A Language and Protocol for Knowledge and Information Exchange (1994); EMAS-S016: Semantics and Verification of Information-Based Protocols (2012); EMAS-S044: Multiagent Commitment Alignment (2009); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Correlate messages with the same interaction and constrain which information may be introduced, repeated or consumed at each local step.

**Development:** Conversation scripts are supplemented or replaced by explicit information dependencies where those better support decentralised enactment.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Correlate messages with the same interaction and constrain which information may be introduced, repeated or consumed at each local step.

**Problem:** Concurrent conversations, delayed messages or reused task parameters.

**Mechanism:** Correlate messages with the same interaction and constrain which information may be introduced, repeated or consumed at each local step.

**Trigger and context:** Concurrent conversations, delayed messages or reused task parameters.

**Preconditions:** Identity binding, data integrity and local history sufficient for the selected protocol.

**Decision or consumer:** Receiver and protocol enactment engine

**Authority boundary:** Participants may produce only information/effects allowed by the interaction contract.

**Communication or interaction:** Separate transport, syntax, ontology, conversation identity, information causality and social effects; choose a stated semantic profile. Specific coupling: Correlate messages with the same interaction and constrain which information may be introduced, repeated or consumed at each local step.

**Observable criterion:** No message silently changes an established binding or discharges a different task’s obligation.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A single interaction with unique values may need only a small correlation key and explicit preconditions.

### EMAS-011 — Protocol enactability and completion possibility

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S016; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S016: Semantics and Verification of Information-Based Protocols (2012); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Check local enactability and the existence of a legal completion from reachable protocol states, using the exact selected definition of liveness.

**Development:** Information-based liveness strengthens mere message legality while leaving operative progress a separate concern.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Check local enactability and the existence of a legal completion from reachable protocol states, using the exact selected definition of liveness.

**Problem:** Protocol-level choices can make otherwise well-typed conversations impossible to finish.

**Mechanism:** Check local enactability and the existence of a legal completion from reachable protocol states, using the exact selected definition of liveness.

**Trigger and context:** Protocol-level choices can make otherwise well-typed conversations impossible to finish.

**Preconditions:** Finite or analysable protocol state; valid information assumptions and a specified environment model.

**Decision or consumer:** Protocol designer

**Authority boundary:** Verifier checks the model; it cannot force participants to complete it.

**Communication or interaction:** Separate transport, syntax, ontology, conversation identity, information causality and social effects; choose a stated semantic profile. Specific coupling: Check local enactability and the existence of a legal completion from reachable protocol states, using the exact selected definition of liveness.

**Observable criterion:** The model has no reachable state without a permitted completion under its liveness definition.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A direct request with a documented terminal alternative may need no heavyweight protocol verification.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Completion possibility is confused with actual eventual willingness, delivery or fulfilled work.

**Mechanism:** Demand evidence appropriate to each claimed transition instead of treating sent or parsed messages as understood, accepted or fulfilled commitments.

**Trigger and context:** A decision depends on another participant having accepted information or done work.

### EMAS-014 — Performatives or role prompts constitute cooperation

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S012; EMAS-S014; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S012: KQML—A Language and Protocol for Knowledge and Information Exchange (1994); EMAS-S014: FIPA Communicative Act Library Specification (2001-08-10); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Development:** Performatives remain useful representational devices when coupled to semantics, not guarantees by themselves.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Reject message labels and role prose as substitutes for an agreed effect, consumer and operative commitment.

**Problem:** Words such as agree, critic or manager are offered as evidence of coordination.

**Mechanism:** Reject message labels and role prose as substitutes for an agreed effect, consumer and operative commitment.

**Trigger and context:** Words such as agree, critic or manager are offered as evidence of coordination.

**Preconditions:** Meaning and authority would have to be established independently of the label.

**Authority boundary:** Prompt author cannot grant a model authority merely by naming a role.

**Communication or interaction:** Separate transport, syntax, ontology, conversation identity, information causality and social effects; choose a stated semantic profile. Specific coupling: Reject message labels and role prose as substitutes for an agreed effect, consumer and operative commitment.

**Observable criterion:** No property is inferred from the label alone; retained behaviour is tested through EMAS-007/008/012.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A plain message with enforceable meaning is better than an elaborate performative vocabulary with none.

### EMAS-015 — Capability- and cost-aware task allocation

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S004; EMAS-S045; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S004: The Contract Net Protocol: High-Level Communication and Control in a Distributed Problem Solver (1980-12); EMAS-S045: An Implementation of the Contract Net Protocol Based on Marginal Cost Calculations (1993); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Use task requirements, current capability, load and marginal bundle cost to decide suitability and assignment; select central or decentralised allocation according to constraints.

**Development:** Original CNP’s task-connection framework receives explicit marginal-cost and task-bundle decision rules.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Use task requirements, current capability, load and marginal bundle cost to decide suitability and assignment; select central or decentralised allocation according to constraints.

**Problem:** There are multiple feasible workers with materially different costs or information.

**Mechanism:** Use task requirements, current capability, load and marginal bundle cost to decide suitability and assignment; select central or decentralised allocation according to constraints.

**Trigger and context:** There are multiple feasible workers with materially different costs or information.

**Preconditions:** Comparable suitability criteria, current capacity and an agreed award/acceptance protocol.

**Decision or consumer:** Allocator and worker

**Authority boundary:** Manager awards only within delegated power; contractor’s acceptance is distinct.

**Communication or interaction:** Announcements, bids, awards, withdrawals and completion reports need task correlation and agreed acceptance semantics. Specific coupling: Use task requirements, current capability, load and marginal bundle cost to decide suitability and assignment; select central or decentralised allocation according to constraints.

**Observable criterion:** The assigned worker can feasibly perform the task under the stated capability and resource assumptions.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Direct assignment to the already authorised capable owner when alternatives cannot improve the outcome.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Stale bids, hidden costs and interacting tasks make a locally cheap award infeasible.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S046; EMAS-S041; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S046; LIMITS: Restricts domains so one agent’s task cost is independent of others’ resource use. Rich contract search, penalty enforcement and strategic information remain substantive costs.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S041; LIMITS: Rule-based kitchen agent simulation, not a production LLM or human organisation study. ### EMAS-016 — Award, acceptance, execution and task completion are distinct

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S004; EMAS-S044; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S004: The Contract Net Protocol: High-Level Communication and Control in a Distributed Problem Solver (1980-12); EMAS-S044: Multiagent Commitment Alignment (2009); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Allocation protocols are coupled to public commitment alignment and outcome verification.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Track the consequential task lifecycle: an award proposes responsibility, acceptance establishes it under the protocol, execution changes objects, and evidence supports completion.

**Problem:** Another party will rely on allocated work or reassign it after apparent failure.

**Mechanism:** Track the consequential task lifecycle: an award proposes responsibility, acceptance establishes it under the protocol, execution changes objects, and evidence supports completion.

**Trigger and context:** Another party will rely on allocated work or reassign it after apparent failure.

**Preconditions:** Task identity, completion criterion, responsible parties and an observation path.

**Decision or consumer:** Task consumer and recovering allocator

**Authority boundary:** Allocator, acceptor and result assessor need not be the same actor.

**Communication or interaction:** Announcements, bids, awards, withdrawals and completion reports need task correlation and agreed acceptance semantics. Specific coupling: Track the consequential task lifecycle: an award proposes responsibility, acceptance establishes it under the protocol, execution changes objects, and evidence supports completion.

**Observable criterion:** Awarded work is not marked fulfilled solely because an award or self-report exists.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A simple shared task record or direct result check can suffice; no contract hierarchy is mandatory.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Premature completion, abandoned awards and duplicate execution on retry.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S036; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S036; LIMITS: 1,642 collected traces across seven frameworks differ from 150 manually grounded-theory traces across five frameworks. ### EMAS-017 — Marginal-cost bargaining improves global allocation under a stated regime

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S045; EMAS-S046; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S045: An Implementation of the Contract Net Protocol Based on Marginal Cost Calculations (1993); EMAS-S046: Issues in Automated Negotiation and Electronic Commerce: Extending the Contract Net Framework (1995); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Accept beneficial task transfers or bundles using the selected pricing, cost and participation model; keep truthfulness and welfare claims separate.

**Development:** Sandholm’s competitive/cooperative extension is narrowed by explicit bounded-rational and strategic analysis.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Accept beneficial task transfers or bundles using the selected pricing, cost and participation model; keep truthfulness and welfare claims separate.

**Problem:** Independent decision makers can trade task responsibility and realise genuine cost savings.

**Mechanism:** Accept beneficial task transfers or bundles using the selected pricing, cost and participation model; keep truthfulness and welfare claims separate.

**Trigger and context:** Independent decision makers can trade task responsibility and realise genuine cost savings.

**Preconditions:** Feasible transfers, modelled cost interactions, enforceable payments and defensible reports or incentives.

**Decision or consumer:** Negotiating parties

**Authority boundary:** Participants may refuse or price transfers; a coordinator cannot assume free truthful cooperation.

**Communication or interaction:** Announcements, bids, awards, withdrawals and completion reports need task correlation and agreed acceptance semantics. Specific coupling: Accept beneficial task transfers or bundles using the selected pricing, cost and participation model; keep truthfulness and welfare claims separate.

**Observable criterion:** Each accepted transfer meets its modelled participation and improvement condition; global optimality is not inferred.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Cooperative cost reporting or direct scheduling where incentives and ownership do not require bargaining.

**Historical disposition:** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Failure modes:** Private costs, strategic bids, externalities and computation limits defeat naive mutual-benefit arguments.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S024; EMAS-S040; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S024; LIMITS: Does not establish an impossibility for every market, domain restriction, subsidy regime or informational setting. No MAS deployment claim.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S040; LIMITS: Two-player discretised pricing simulation. Known cost types in meta-analysis and restricted strategy set limit transfer.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Private costs, strategic bids, externalities and computation limits defeat naive mutual-benefit arguments.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.

### EMAS-018 — Partial global planning with revisable local views

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S006; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S006: Using Partial Global Plans to Coordinate Distributed Problem Solvers (1987); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Share selected local plans and dependencies to construct revisable partial global plans while planning and execution continue.

**Development:** PGP’s central and distributed organisations remain selectable alternatives, not a universal decentralisation preference.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Share selected local plans and dependencies to construct revisable partial global plans while planning and execution continue.

**Problem:** Agents’ activities interact over time and purely local plans create avoidable conflict or missed cooperation.

**Mechanism:** Share selected local plans and dependencies to construct revisable partial global plans while planning and execution continue.

**Trigger and context:** Agents’ activities interact over time and purely local plans create avoidable conflict or missed cooperation.

**Preconditions:** Communicable summaries, a useful interdependence model and rules for reconciling changed plans.

**Decision or consumer:** Local planners and coordination peers

**Authority boundary:** Plan summaries advise or constrain within the selected coordination arrangement.

**Communication or interaction:** Announcements, bids, awards, withdrawals and completion reports need task correlation and agreed acceptance semantics. Specific coupling: Share selected local plans and dependencies to construct revisable partial global plans while planning and execution continue.

**Observable criterion:** Relevant agents adjust timing or activity selection based on shared dependencies rather than assuming a complete world plan.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Independent execution or a fixed central schedule when dependencies are stable and cheap to resolve.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Stale views can cause redundant work or mutual deferral; communication overhead can dominate simple cases.

<a id="emas-019"></a>
### EMAS-019 — Dependency elimination can substitute for coordination

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S006; EMAS-S045; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S006: Using Partial Global Plans to Coordinate Distributed Problem Solvers (1987); EMAS-S045: An Implementation of the Contract Net Protocol Based on Marginal Cost Calculations (1993); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Task clustering and communication-cost criticism support alternatives to ever-richer protocols.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Before adding negotiation, restructure tasks, bundle interactions or partition resources where this removes the relevant dependency at acceptable cost.

**Problem:** Repeated communication exists only to manage an avoidable coupling.

**Mechanism:** Before adding negotiation, restructure tasks, bundle interactions or partition resources where this removes the relevant dependency at acceptable cost.

**Trigger and context:** Repeated communication exists only to manage an avoidable coupling.

**Preconditions:** The dependency can be removed without losing required cross-boundary rights, quality or capacity.

**Decision or consumer:** Decomposition designer

**Authority boundary:** Resource owners must authorise altered partitions; designers cannot erase external interdependence by drawing boundaries.

**Communication or interaction:** Announcements, bids, awards, withdrawals and completion reports need task correlation and agreed acceptance semantics. Specific coupling: Before adding negotiation, restructure tasks, bundle interactions or partition resources where this removes the relevant dependency at acceptable cost.

**Observable criterion:** The original conflict no longer arises, or its residual scope is smaller and explicit.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** No additional coordination when independently executable tasks already meet the collective objective.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Overpartitioning wastes shared capacity; bundling can reduce flexibility and increase local complexity.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S041; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S041; LIMITS: Rule-based kitchen agent simulation, not a production LLM or human organisation study. <a id="emas-020"></a>
### EMAS-020 — Shared-resource and temporal dependency constraints

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S006; EMAS-S028; EMAS-S045; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S006: Using Partial Global Plans to Coordinate Distributed Problem Solvers (1987); EMAS-S028: A Scalable Method for Multiagent Constraint Optimization (2005); EMAS-S045: An Implementation of the Contract Net Protocol Based on Marginal Cost Calculations (1993); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Planning, constraint optimisation and contemporary handover benchmarks expose different forms of the same resource interdependence.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Represent the resource and timing conditions that make local task choices jointly feasible, then enforce or coordinate those conditions through an appropriate allocator or protocol.

**Problem:** Tasks contend for a resource, require synchrony or have precedence and handover windows.

**Mechanism:** Represent the resource and timing conditions that make local task choices jointly feasible, then enforce or coordinate those conditions through an appropriate allocator or protocol.

**Trigger and context:** Tasks contend for a resource, require synchrony or have precedence and handover windows.

**Preconditions:** Identifiable resources, feasibility predicates and a mechanism that reaches the actors actually controlling them.

**Decision or consumer:** Scheduler and resource participants

**Authority boundary:** Possession and permission to reserve/use a resource must be distinguished.

**Communication or interaction:** Announcements, bids, awards, withdrawals and completion reports need task correlation and agreed acceptance semantics. Specific coupling: Represent the resource and timing conditions that make local task choices jointly feasible, then enforce or coordinate those conditions through an appropriate allocator or protocol.

**Observable criterion:** Local assignments compose into a feasible joint resource/time configuration and its execution is observed.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Local ownership, reserved capacity or a fixed schedule when sufficient and cheaper.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Optimised allocation omits real contention or fails during an unobserved handover.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S046; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S046; LIMITS: Restricts domains so one agent’s task cost is independent of others’ resource use. Rich contract search, penalty enforcement and strategic information remain substantive costs.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Optimised allocation omits real contention or fails during an unobserved handover.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.

**Contrary evidence:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S046; ADJUDICATION: Optimised allocation omits real contention or fails during an unobserved handover.; NOT_A_REFUTATION_OF: A bounded theorem whose actual assumptions remain satisfied.

**Assumption sensitivity:** MATERIAL_ASSUMPTIONS: Identifiable resources, feasibility predicates and a mechanism that reaches the actors actually controlling them.; CHANGE_THAT_REOPENS_JUDGEMENT: Which physical or external timing constraint is missing from the model?

**Unresolved questions:** Which physical or external timing constraint is missing from the model?

<a id="emas-021"></a>
### EMAS-021 — Conditional decommitment and recontracting

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S046; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S046: Issues in Automated Negotiation and Electronic Commerce: Extending the Contract Net Framework (1995); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Allow explicitly priced or otherwise agreed withdrawal and renegotiation where uncertainty makes irrevocable commitment inefficient.

**Development:** Conditional penalties address the inflexibility of full commitment without promising free exit.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Allow explicitly priced or otherwise agreed withdrawal and renegotiation where uncertainty makes irrevocable commitment inefficient.

**Problem:** New information changes the value or feasibility of a contract after commitment.

**Mechanism:** Allow explicitly priced or otherwise agreed withdrawal and renegotiation where uncertainty makes irrevocable commitment inefficient.

**Trigger and context:** New information changes the value or feasibility of a contract after commitment.

**Preconditions:** Withdrawal conditions, authority, compensation and notification rules that the parties can actually enforce.

**Decision or consumer:** Contract parties and dependent task owners

**Authority boundary:** Decommitting party cannot unilaterally erase obligations outside the agreed contract.

**Communication or interaction:** Announcements, bids, awards, withdrawals and completion reports need task correlation and agreed acceptance semantics. Specific coupling: Allow explicitly priced or otherwise agreed withdrawal and renegotiation where uncertainty makes irrevocable commitment inefficient.

**Observable criterion:** Withdrawal changes the obligation under agreed rules rather than silently abandoning dependent work.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Firm commitments for safety-critical or indivisible dependencies; simple cancellation by mutual consent where enough.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Penalties can be unenforceable, strategically exploited or smaller than third-party losses.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S024; EMAS-S044; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S024; LIMITS: Does not establish an impossibility for every market, domain restriction, subsidy regime or informational setting. No MAS deployment claim.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S044; LIMITS: Guarantee depends on observation/channel and behavioural integrity assumptions; quiescent alignment is not instantaneous omniscience, enforced honesty or task fulfilment.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Penalties can be unenforceable, strategically exploited or smaller than third-party losses.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.

**Unresolved questions:** Who bears a withdrawal loss not priced by the bilateral contract?

<a id="emas-022"></a>
### EMAS-022 — Negotiate every task

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S004; EMAS-S045; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S004: The Contract Net Protocol: High-Level Communication and Control in a Distributed Problem Solver (1980-12); EMAS-S045: An Implementation of the Contract Net Protocol Based on Marginal Cost Calculations (1993); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Costly negotiation is treated as an option, not the essence of all coordination.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Reject compulsory negotiation when ownership and a suitable executor are already determined.

**Problem:** Protocol use is defended solely because the system is multi-agent.

**Mechanism:** Reject compulsory negotiation when ownership and a suitable executor are already determined.

**Trigger and context:** Protocol use is defended solely because the system is multi-agent.

**Preconditions:** Negotiation would need a genuine allocative or consent decision to improve.

**Decision or consumer:** Allocator

**Authority boundary:** The existing authorised owner need not seek a new auction winner.

**Communication or interaction:** Announcements, bids, awards, withdrawals and completion reports need task correlation and agreed acceptance semantics. Specific coupling: Reject compulsory negotiation when ownership and a suitable executor are already determined.

**Observable criterion:** Negotiation is retained only where its expected value exceeds its time, communication and strategic cost.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Direct authorised execution or a fixed rule without bids.

**Historical disposition:** REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED

**Failure modes:** Bidding delays useful work and creates a failure point without changing the decision.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S006; EMAS-S046; EMAS-S041; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S006; LIMITS: Distributed vehicle-monitoring simulation, four-node difficult scenario; original meta-organisation is specified in advance. No numerical effect extracted from its results table. Rich contract search, penalty enforcement and strategic information remain substantive costs.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S041; LIMITS: Rule-based kitchen agent simulation, not a production LLM or human organisation study. <a id="emas-023"></a>
### EMAS-023 — Joint-goal persistence with explicit reconsideration

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S007; EMAS-S011; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S007: On Acting Together (1990); EMAS-S011: Commitments and conventions: The foundation of coordination in multi-agent systems (1993); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Maintain a joint undertaking until recognised achievement, impossibility or irrelevance triggers justified reconsideration and notification.

**Development:** Logical persistence is coupled to conventions, selective communication and operational repair.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Maintain a joint undertaking until recognised achievement, impossibility or irrelevance triggers justified reconsideration and notification.

**Problem:** Individual withdrawal would leave other participants relying on a continuing team commitment.

**Mechanism:** Maintain a joint undertaking until recognised achievement, impossibility or irrelevance triggers justified reconsideration and notification.

**Trigger and context:** Individual withdrawal would leave other participants relying on a continuing team commitment.

**Preconditions:** Joint objective, participant set, persistence conditions and a viable notification path.

**Decision or consumer:** Team members

**Authority boundary:** A participant may recognise changed conditions but must respect the undertaking’s termination rules.

**Communication or interaction:** Team communication can notify achievement, impossibility, irrelevance, delegation or repair; some changes require recipients rather than broadcast. Specific coupling: Maintain a joint undertaking until recognised achievement, impossibility or irrelevance triggers justified reconsideration and notification.

**Observable criterion:** An agent does not silently cease contributing while others still reasonably rely on the joint undertaking.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Independent work needs no joint-intention machinery; short atomic cooperation may need only an explicit completion/abort contract.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Stubborn persistence under harmful goals; cost-prohibitive attempts to establish ideal mutual belief.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S010; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S010; LIMITS: Finite decision model and controlled settings; does not settle all semantic accounts of collective intention.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Stubborn persistence under harmful goals; cost-prohibitive attempts to establish ideal mutual belief.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.

### EMAS-024 — Partial SharedPlans and capability-directed collaboration

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S008; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S008: Collaborative Plans for Complex Group Action (1996); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Relate individual and subgroup plans, recipe knowledge and intentions towards others’ ability to perform their contributions; allow partial elaboration.

**Development:** Partial and full SharedPlans remain distinct; added structure is not a claim of omniscience.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Relate individual and subgroup plans, recipe knowledge and intentions towards others’ ability to perform their contributions; allow partial elaboration.

**Problem:** A joint activity needs coordinated subactivities whose details cannot all be fixed initially.

**Mechanism:** Relate individual and subgroup plans, recipe knowledge and intentions towards others’ ability to perform their contributions; allow partial elaboration.

**Trigger and context:** A joint activity needs coordinated subactivities whose details cannot all be fixed initially.

**Preconditions:** Understandable action recipes, relevant capabilities and reconciliation of subplan obligations.

**Decision or consumer:** Team planner

**Authority boundary:** Each actor is responsible for its contribution under the selected team agreement.

**Communication or interaction:** Team communication can notify achievement, impossibility, irrelevance, delegation or repair; some changes require recipients rather than broadcast. Specific coupling: Relate individual and subgroup plans, recipe knowledge and intentions towards others’ ability to perform their contributions; allow partial elaboration.

**Observable criterion:** Unknown plan components remain explicitly partial rather than being mistaken for a completed executable plan.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** An agreed simple task split or direct request where the richer intentional model adds no decision value.

<a id="emas-025"></a>
### EMAS-025 — Public conditional commitments

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S013; EMAS-S044; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S013: Agent Communication Languages: Rethinking the Principles (1998-12); EMAS-S044: Multiagent Commitment Alignment (2009); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Represent who owes what to whom under which antecedent, and define create, detach, discharge, release and cancellation effects publicly.

**Development:** Social commitment semantics replaces opaque sincerity as the public accountability object, not as a universal theory of cognition.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Represent who owes what to whom under which antecedent, and define create, detach, discharge, release and cancellation effects publicly.

**Problem:** Open parties must reason about obligations without inspecting one another’s implementation.

**Mechanism:** Represent who owes what to whom under which antecedent, and define create, detach, discharge, release and cancellation effects publicly.

**Trigger and context:** Open parties must reason about obligations without inspecting one another’s implementation.

**Preconditions:** Creditor/debtor identity, agreed antecedent/consequent meanings and observable lifecycle events.

**Decision or consumer:** Counterparties and accountability observer

**Authority boundary:** Debtor, creditor and authorised third parties have different lifecycle powers.

**Communication or interaction:** Team communication can notify achievement, impossibility, irrelevance, delegation or repair; some changes require recipients rather than broadcast. Specific coupling: Represent who owes what to whom under which antecedent, and define create, detach, discharge, release and cancellation effects publicly.

**Observable criterion:** Participants can distinguish a conditional obligation from an activated or fulfilled one.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A direct observable exchange or ordinary contract state when the conditional formalism adds no clarity.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** An apparent antecedent or reported consequence may be false in the external world; norms can conflict.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S021; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S021; LIMITS: Constructed regulated water-market scenario, not evidence of real-world judicial enforcement. The 2010 event and 2011 publication dates are distinct. ### EMAS-026 — Commitment alignment and bounded delegation

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S044; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S044: Multiagent Commitment Alignment (2009); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Ensure parties receive the information required to maintain compatible commitment views; delegation changes responsibilities only through specified party-aware operations.

**Development:** Alignment formalises the communication obligations latent in commitment lifecycle changes.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Ensure parties receive the information required to maintain compatible commitment views; delegation changes responsibilities only through specified party-aware operations.

**Problem:** Commitments are distributed, strengthened, cancelled, assigned or delegated.

**Mechanism:** Ensure parties receive the information required to maintain compatible commitment views; delegation changes responsibilities only through specified party-aware operations.

**Trigger and context:** Commitments are distributed, strengthened, cancelled, assigned or delegated.

**Preconditions:** Reliable ordered messaging and integrity rules for the cited theorem, or a separately analysed replacement transport model.

**Decision or consumer:** Creditor, debtor and delegate

**Authority boundary:** Delegating work does not necessarily release the original debtor; that depends on the agreed operation.

**Communication or interaction:** Team communication can notify achievement, impossibility, irrelevance, delegation or repair; some changes require recipients rather than broadcast. Specific coupling: Ensure parties receive the information required to maintain compatible commitment views; delegation changes responsibilities only through specified party-aware operations.

**Observable criterion:** At the model’s alignment boundary, creditor expectations do not exceed the debtor’s recognised obligation without the required information.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A single shared authoritative record or no delegation when it meets the same visibility and autonomy constraints.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** In-flight messages, silent role transfer or hidden subcontracting strand expectations.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S047; EMAS-S021; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S047; LIMITS: Model-dependent impossibility; weaker coordination objectives or different delivery assumptions must be analysed separately. <a id="emas-027"></a>
### EMAS-027 — Team monitoring, notification and repair

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S009; EMAS-S011; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S009: Towards Flexible Teamwork (1997); EMAS-S011: Commitments and conventions: The foundation of coordination in multi-agent systems (1993); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Observe consequential progress and changed assumptions, notify dependent teammates, and choose repair, reassignment or justified termination.

**Development:** STEAM and mixed-initiative systems operationalise repair beyond descriptive shared intentions.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Observe consequential progress and changed assumptions, notify dependent teammates, and choose repair, reassignment or justified termination.

**Problem:** A required contribution fails or a plan premise changes.

**Mechanism:** Observe consequential progress and changed assumptions, notify dependent teammates, and choose repair, reassignment or justified termination.

**Trigger and context:** A required contribution fails or a plan premise changes.

**Preconditions:** Observable failure signals, identifiable dependants and an authorised repair action.

**Decision or consumer:** Team coordinator and affected member

**Authority boundary:** Repair authority is limited by the task and participant agreement; changed goals may need human decision.

**Communication or interaction:** Team communication can notify achievement, impossibility, irrelevance, delegation or repair; some changes require recipients rather than broadcast. Specific coupling: Observe consequential progress and changed assumptions, notify dependent teammates, and choose repair, reassignment or justified termination.

**Observable criterion:** Outstanding obligations are either restored, explicitly changed or visibly left unresolved.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Direct retry or a clear abort may be adequate for a short task with no lingering dependency.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** False failure diagnosis, duplicated action and endless replanning can degrade progress.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S008; EMAS-S010; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S008; LIMITS: Formal account does not itself supply continuous action monitoring; resource conflicts can remain unrecognised. No general deployment-effect claim.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S010; LIMITS: Finite decision model and controlled settings; does not settle all semantic accounts of collective intention.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: False failure diagnosis, duplicated action and endless replanning can degrade progress.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.

**Unresolved questions:** Does repair restore useful work or merely restart the same failing conversation?

<a id="emas-028"></a>
### EMAS-028 — Selective decision-theoretic communication

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S009; EMAS-S010; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S009: Towards Flexible Teamwork (1997); EMAS-S010: Team Coordination among Distributed Agents: Analyzing Key Teamwork Theories and Models (2002); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Communicate when expected coordination value outweighs communication and interruption costs, while honouring mandatory notification obligations.

**Development:** Teamwork theory is narrowed by explicit communication costs rather than maximal belief-sharing as default.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Communicate when expected coordination value outweighs communication and interruption costs, while honouring mandatory notification obligations.

**Problem:** Communication is costly, distracting or constrained and recipients need different information.

**Mechanism:** Communicate when expected coordination value outweighs communication and interruption costs, while honouring mandatory notification obligations.

**Trigger and context:** Communication is costly, distracting or constrained and recipients need different information.

**Preconditions:** A defensible model of information value and cost, plus non-negotiable safety/commitment notices.

**Decision or consumer:** Communicating team member

**Authority boundary:** Sender controls optional sharing but cannot price away a binding notification duty.

**Communication or interaction:** Team communication can notify achievement, impossibility, irrelevance, delegation or repair; some changes require recipients rather than broadcast. Specific coupling: Communicate when expected coordination value outweighs communication and interruption costs, while honouring mandatory notification obligations.

**Observable criterion:** The chosen policy reduces unnecessary exchange without withholding information required for coordination.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Always send a cheap critical notification; use a fixed minimal protocol when estimating value costs more than it saves.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Incorrect value estimates suppress a rare essential warning; unrestricted broadcasting overloads recipients.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S006; EMAS-S047; EMAS-S054; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S006; LIMITS: Distributed vehicle-monitoring simulation, four-node difficult scenario; original meta-organisation is specified in advance. No numerical effect extracted from its results table. Simpler situations can lose from coordination overhead.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S047; LIMITS: Model-dependent impossibility; weaker coordination objectives or different delivery assumptions must be analysed separately. Imported distributed-computing result, not a claim every MAS requires common knowledge.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S054; LIMITS: 38 analysed participants from 41 convenience recruits; control/text/audio groups 14/14/10, four 80-second sessions on one layout, gaming-experienced sample. Underpowered and non-significant results do not establish equivalence or no benefit in all populations. Exploratory adaptation patterns are not confirmed effects.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Incorrect value estimates suppress a rare essential warning; unrestricted broadcasting overloads recipients. A coordination-relevant explanation must still be useful to its actual human recipient; transparent status narration has not earned universal benefit.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.

**Contrary evidence:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S006; EMAS-S047; EMAS-S054; ADJUDICATION: Incorrect value estimates suppress a rare essential warning; unrestricted broadcasting overloads recipients.; NOT_A_REFUTATION_OF: A bounded theorem whose actual assumptions remain satisfied.

### EMAS-030 — Strategic model and solution-concept selection

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S023; EMAS-S024; EMAS-S025; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S023: Multiagent Systems: Algorithmic, Game-Theoretic, and Logical Foundations (2009; manuscript revision also ©2010); EMAS-S024: Efficient Mechanisms for Bilateral Trading (1983); EMAS-S025: Manipulation of Voting Schemes: A General Result (1973); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Economic imports become MAS tools through explicit computational and informational modelling.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Select the game, information structure and solution concept that match the interaction; state what equilibrium or incentive claim means and excludes.

**Problem:** Participants have potentially conflicting objectives and can choose their responses.

**Mechanism:** Select the game, information structure and solution concept that match the interaction; state what equilibrium or incentive claim means and excludes.

**Trigger and context:** Participants have potentially conflicting objectives and can choose their responses.

**Preconditions:** Feasible actions, payoffs or preference model, information and participation assumptions.

**Decision or consumer:** Strategic-system analyst

**Authority boundary:** A mechanism designer proposes rules; participants retain their available actions and outside options.

**Communication or interaction:** Bids and offers are strategic messages within a mechanism, not automatically accurate statements of need or cost. Specific coupling: Select the game, information structure and solution concept that match the interaction; state what equilibrium or incentive claim means and excludes.

**Observable criterion:** The advertised strategic prediction is stated under the model that supports it.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Cooperative planning when conflicts are absent and compliance is justified; no equilibrium computation for its own sake.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** One computed equilibrium is non-unique, inaccessible or irrelevant to bounded actors.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S046; EMAS-S040; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S046; LIMITS: Restricts domains so one agent’s task cost is independent of others’ resource use. Rich contract search, penalty enforcement and strategic information remain substantive costs.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S040; LIMITS: Two-player discretised pricing simulation. Tabular pretraining: 500 games/1,000 policies; LLM comparison selects six GPT5-mini meta-strategies, 40 base-game samples, 50 rounds, four prices. Known cost types in meta-analysis and restricted strategy set limit transfer.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: One computed equilibrium is non-unique, inaccessible or irrelevant to bounded actors.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.

**Contrary evidence:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S046; EMAS-S040; ADJUDICATION: One computed equilibrium is non-unique, inaccessible or irrelevant to bounded actors.; NOT_A_REFUTATION_OF: A bounded theorem whose actual assumptions remain satisfied.

**Assumption sensitivity:** MATERIAL_ASSUMPTIONS: Feasible actions, payoffs or preference model, information and participation assumptions.; CHANGE_THAT_REOPENS_JUDGEMENT: Which change in beliefs or feasible deviations overturns the selected solution?

**Unresolved questions:** Which change in beliefs or feasible deviations overturns the selected solution?

**Property versus ceremony:** MINIMAL_ADEQUATE_FORM: Cooperative planning when conflicts are absent and compliance is justified; no equilibrium computation for its own sake.; ACTUAL_CONSUMER: Strategic-system analyst; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT: Omit or retire when the stated trigger/dependency is absent and the observable postcondition is already secured by a cheaper adequate arrangement; preserve outstanding obligations during any transition.; FALSE_SUBSTITUTE: A representation or label without the stated postcondition and consumer does not establish this candidate.

### EMAS-031 — Incentive-compatible reporting mechanisms

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S023; EMAS-S024; EMAS-S025; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S023: Multiagent Systems: Algorithmic, Game-Theoretic, and Logical Foundations (2009; manuscript revision also ©2010); EMAS-S024: Efficient Mechanisms for Bilateral Trading (1983); EMAS-S025: Manipulation of Voting Schemes: A General Result (1973); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Design allocation and payment rules so truthful reporting or the desired action is optimal under the declared incentive concept and domain.

**Development:** Inherited mechanism-design guarantees are retained with their domains rather than equated with fairness or ethics.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Design allocation and payment rules so truthful reporting or the desired action is optimal under the declared incentive concept and domain.

**Problem:** Private information matters and inaccurate reports can improve a participant’s payoff.

**Mechanism:** Design allocation and payment rules so truthful reporting or the desired action is optimal under the declared incentive concept and domain.

**Trigger and context:** Private information matters and inaccurate reports can improve a participant’s payoff.

**Preconditions:** Declared type domain, transfer powers, utility assumptions and the exact equilibrium or dominance condition.

**Decision or consumer:** Mechanism operator and participant

**Authority boundary:** Mechanism operator must possess the advertised allocation/payment authority.

**Communication or interaction:** Bids and offers are strategic messages within a mechanism, not automatically accurate statements of need or cost. Specific coupling: Design allocation and payment rules so truthful reporting or the desired action is optimal under the declared incentive concept and domain.

**Observable criterion:** No profitable deviation of the specified kind exists within the analysed model.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Observed verifiable inputs or trusted cooperative reporting when adequate and less costly.

**Historical disposition:** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Failure modes:** Collusion, externalities, non-modelled values, approximate computation or unenforceable payments defeat the claim.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S040; EMAS-S046; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S040; LIMITS: Two-player discretised pricing simulation. ### EMAS-032 — Participation, budget and efficiency feasibility

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S024; EMAS-S023; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S024: Efficient Mechanisms for Bilateral Trading (1983); EMAS-S023: Multiagent Systems: Algorithmic, Game-Theoretic, and Logical Foundations (2009; manuscript revision also ©2010); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Impossibility results narrow feasible design promises rather than declaring all negotiation futile.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Check whether participation, budget, efficiency and informational requirements can coexist before optimising or promising all of them.

**Problem:** A mechanism claims voluntary participation, truthful revelation and efficient allocation without subsidy.

**Mechanism:** Check whether participation, budget, efficiency and informational requirements can coexist before optimising or promising all of them.

**Trigger and context:** A mechanism claims voluntary participation, truthful revelation and efficient allocation without subsidy.

**Preconditions:** Exact formal model, including timing of participation and whose costs/budget are counted.

**Decision or consumer:** Mechanism designer

**Authority boundary:** Only authorised stakeholders may relax budget or participation constraints.

**Communication or interaction:** Bids and offers are strategic messages within a mechanism, not automatically accurate statements of need or cost. Specific coupling: Check whether participation, budget, efficiency and informational requirements can coexist before optimising or promising all of them.

**Observable criterion:** Incompatible requirements are exposed and any relaxation is stated to affected parties.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Relax an explicit requirement or restrict the domain rather than hide an impossibility with implementation detail.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Interim and ex-post participation conditions are conflated; a subsidy or distributional loss is omitted.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S024; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S024; LIMITS: Does not establish an impossibility for every market, domain restriction, subsidy regime or informational setting.
