### EMAS-033 — Equilibrium, welfare, fairness and legitimacy remain distinct

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S023; EMAS-S024; EMAS-S022; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S023: Multiagent Systems: Algorithmic, Game-Theoretic, and Logical Foundations (2009; manuscript revision also ©2010); EMAS-S024: Efficient Mechanisms for Bilateral Trading (1983); EMAS-S022: Multi-Agent Systems: Technical & Ethical Challenges of Functioning in a Mixed Group (2022); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Strategic cooperation is not assimilated to benevolent teamwork.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Report strategic stability separately from efficiency, distributional acceptability and external authorisation.

**Problem:** An equilibrium or incentive result is offered as proof of a socially desirable outcome.

**Mechanism:** Report strategic stability separately from efficiency, distributional acceptability and external authorisation.

**Trigger and context:** An equilibrium or incentive result is offered as proof of a socially desirable outcome.

**Preconditions:** Declared welfare/distribution criteria and independent authority boundary.

**Decision or consumer:** Stakeholder and strategic reviewer

**Authority boundary:** External institutions determine legitimacy; analysts specify the game’s objective.

**Communication or interaction:** Bids and offers are strategic messages within a mechanism, not automatically accurate statements of need or cost. Specific coupling: Report strategic stability separately from efficiency, distributional acceptability and external authorisation.

**Observable criterion:** A stable harmful outcome is recognised as such rather than relabelled successful coordination.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A feasible authorised rule may be selected without claiming equilibrium optimality.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Algorithmic collusion can benefit participants while harming outsiders; a legal claim is smuggled into utility.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S040; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S040; LIMITS: Two-player discretised pricing simulation. ### EMAS-034 — Deviation, collusion and opponent-shift testing

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S023; EMAS-S046; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S023: Multiagent Systems: Algorithmic, Game-Theoretic, and Logical Foundations (2009; manuscript revision also ©2010); EMAS-S046: Issues in Automated Negotiation and Electronic Commerce: Extending the Contract Net Framework (1995); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Meta-game and con-resistant evaluations make the test-time strategic population explicit.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Test profitable deviations, coalition behaviour and different opponents rather than only compliant training partners.

**Problem:** Open or mixed-motive interaction or adaptive strategic participants.

**Mechanism:** Test profitable deviations, coalition behaviour and different opponents rather than only compliant training partners.

**Trigger and context:** Open or mixed-motive interaction or adaptive strategic participants.

**Preconditions:** A defined adversarial strategy space, participant incentives and evaluation horizon.

**Decision or consumer:** Strategic evaluator

**Authority boundary:** Testers simulate deviations; they do not receive authority to exploit real counterparties.

**Communication or interaction:** Bids and offers are strategic messages within a mechanism, not automatically accurate statements of need or cost. Specific coupling: Test profitable deviations, coalition behaviour and different opponents rather than only compliant training partners.

**Observable criterion:** Claims distinguish within-partner success from robustness across deviations and opponents.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Basic compliance tests suffice for a truly controlled cooperative population, with that boundary explicit.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** A restricted tested strategy set misses profitable attacks; long-horizon training hides short-horizon exploitation.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S040; EMAS-S049; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S040; LIMITS: Two-player discretised pricing simulation. ### EMAS-035 — Contract and coalition search scope limits

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S046; EMAS-S023; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S046: Issues in Automated Negotiation and Electronic Commerce: Extending the Contract Net Framework (1995); EMAS-S023: Multiagent Systems: Algorithmic, Game-Theoretic, and Logical Foundations (2009; manuscript revision also ©2010); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Choose allowable task bundles and multi-party contracts in relation to the local optima they can escape and the search, information and consent cost they introduce.

**Development:** Linking tasks and multi-agent contracts generalises simple CNP exchanges while preserving bounded-rational limits.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Choose allowable task bundles and multi-party contracts in relation to the local optima they can escape and the search, information and consent cost they introduce.

**Problem:** Bilateral or single-task exchanges stop despite a feasible multi-party improvement.

**Mechanism:** Choose allowable task bundles and multi-party contracts in relation to the local optima they can escape and the search, information and consent cost they introduce.

**Trigger and context:** Bilateral or single-task exchanges stop despite a feasible multi-party improvement.

**Preconditions:** Specified externalities, utilities, feasible coalition agreements and computational budget.

**Decision or consumer:** Negotiation designer

**Authority boundary:** Each required party must validly agree; one organiser cannot consent for all.

**Communication or interaction:** Bids and offers are strategic messages within a mechanism, not automatically accurate statements of need or cost. Specific coupling: Choose allowable task bundles and multi-party contracts in relation to the local optima they can escape and the search, information and consent cost they introduce.

**Observable criterion:** The chosen search neighbourhood and stopping claim are explicit; local termination is not called global optimum.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Keep simple bilateral trades when richer contracts offer no material improvement.

**Historical disposition:** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Failure modes:** Combinatorial search, strategic vetoes and unpriced externalities undermine the expected improvement.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S046; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S046; LIMITS: Restricts domains so one agent’s task cost is independent of others’ resource use. Rich contract search, penalty enforcement and strategic information remain substantive costs.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Combinatorial search, strategic vetoes and unpriced externalities undermine the expected improvement.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.

**Contrary evidence:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S046; ADJUDICATION: Combinatorial search, strategic vetoes and unpriced externalities undermine the expected improvement.; NOT_A_REFUTATION_OF: A bounded theorem whose actual assumptions remain satisfied.

**Assumption sensitivity:** MATERIAL_ASSUMPTIONS: Specified externalities, utilities, feasible coalition agreements and computational budget.; CHANGE_THAT_REOPENS_JUDGEMENT: Which omitted contract type could improve the final allocation?

**Unresolved questions:** Which omitted contract type could improve the final allocation?

**Property versus ceremony:** MINIMAL_ADEQUATE_FORM: Keep simple bilateral trades when richer contracts offer no material improvement.; ACTUAL_CONSUMER: Negotiation designer; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT: Omit or retire when the stated trigger/dependency is absent and the observable postcondition is already secured by a cheaper adequate arrangement; preserve outstanding obligations during any transition.; FALSE_SUBSTITUTE: A representation or label without the stated postcondition and consumer does not establish this candidate.

### EMAS-036 — Couple organisational structure, function and norms

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S018; EMAS-S019; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S018: The Gaia Methodology for Agent-Oriented Analysis and Design (2000); EMAS-S019: MOISE+: towards a structural, functional, and deontic model for MAS organization (2002); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Relate role structure and compatibility to goals/missions and to permissions or obligations, instead of maintaining disconnected organisational diagrams.

**Development:** MOISE+ distinguishes and connects structural, functional and deontic dimensions; normative execution adds further obligations.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Relate role structure and compatibility to goals/missions and to permissions or obligations, instead of maintaining disconnected organisational diagrams.

**Problem:** Several role players must coordinate a persistent organisation with differentiated rights and responsibilities.

**Mechanism:** Relate role structure and compatibility to goals/missions and to permissions or obligations, instead of maintaining disconnected organisational diagrams.

**Trigger and context:** Several role players must coordinate a persistent organisation with differentiated rights and responsibilities.

**Preconditions:** Interpretable goals, role definitions and a mechanism linking role adoption to actual responsibilities.

**Decision or consumer:** Organisation designer and participant

**Authority boundary:** Role adoption and mission acceptance follow organisational rules, not unilateral diagram edits.

**Communication or interaction:** Role adoption, mission acceptance, normative changes and violations have identifiable communicative or environmental evidence. Specific coupling: Relate role structure and compatibility to goals/missions and to permissions or obligations, instead of maintaining disconnected organisational diagrams.

**Observable criterion:** An actor’s organisational role connects to identifiable permitted or required contributions.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A small direct responsibility agreement for a small team; no comprehensive organisational ontology is mandatory.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Well-formed diagrams conceal missing performers or unreachable organisational goals.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S020; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S020; LIMITS: The model assumes actions observable and effects determined by the norm artefact. A simulated fine does not establish legal power over real people. ### EMAS-037 — Norm declaration, detection and enforcement are distinct

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S020; EMAS-S021; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S020: Normative Multi-Agent Programs and Their Logics (2008); EMAS-S021: Norm Enforceability in Electronic Institutions? (2011 (published volume); COIN 2010 event); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Normative programming and enforcement criticism separate representational rules from their operative reach.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** For each consequential norm, distinguish the rule, observable violation condition and feasible response; disclose norms whose violations remain unobservable.

**Problem:** Compliance with obligations or prohibitions is relied upon.

**Mechanism:** For each consequential norm, distinguish the rule, observable violation condition and feasible response; disclose norms whose violations remain unobservable.

**Trigger and context:** Compliance with obligations or prohibitions is relied upon.

**Preconditions:** Norm meaning, observable facts, legitimate response powers and a fallible-observation policy.

**Decision or consumer:** Norm issuer, participant and enforcer

**Authority boundary:** Monitors establish observations; only authorised enforcers impose permitted consequences.

**Communication or interaction:** Role adoption, mission acceptance, normative changes and violations have identifiable communicative or environmental evidence. Specific coupling: For each consequential norm, distinguish the rule, observable violation condition and feasible response; disclose norms whose violations remain unobservable.

**Observable criterion:** The system states what it can prevent, detect, sanction or only request.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A simple physical or software constraint may eliminate the violation; declaration-only norms are not enforcement evidence.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Unseen external acts and ambiguous rules render a norm operationally inert or falsely enforced.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S021; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S021; LIMITS: Constructed regulated water-market scenario, not evidence of real-world judicial enforcement. The 2010 event and 2011 publication dates are distinct. ### EMAS-038 — Select regimentation, enforcement or voluntary norm reasoning

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S020; EMAS-S021; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S020: Normative Multi-Agent Programs and Their Logics (2008); EMAS-S021: Norm Enforceability in Electronic Institutions? (2011 (published volume); COIN 2010 event); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Choose prevention, post-violation response or agent-side deliberation according to observability, autonomy, costs and authorised powers.

**Development:** These remain incompatible-in-part options, not stages every system must accumulate.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Choose prevention, post-violation response or agent-side deliberation according to observability, autonomy, costs and authorised powers.

**Problem:** An organisation must decide how strongly to constrain behaviour.

**Mechanism:** Choose prevention, post-violation response or agent-side deliberation according to observability, autonomy, costs and authorised powers.

**Trigger and context:** An organisation must decide how strongly to constrain behaviour.

**Preconditions:** Action mediation for prevention, observation/response for sanctions, or interpretable norms for deliberation.

**Decision or consumer:** Institution designer

**Authority boundary:** Institutional power bounds all three choices; a simulated fine is not real authority.

**Communication or interaction:** Role adoption, mission acceptance, normative changes and violations have identifiable communicative or environmental evidence. Specific coupling: Choose prevention, post-violation response or agent-side deliberation according to observability, autonomy, costs and authorised powers.

**Observable criterion:** The selected mechanism’s coverage and retained freedom are explicit.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** No added enforcement when voluntary behaviour and low consequence make it unnecessary; a direct constraint when safe and sufficient.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Prevention blocks legitimate exceptions; sanctions arrive too late; voluntary reasoning does not assure compliance.

### EMAS-039 — Role adoption and capability/permission compatibility

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S018; EMAS-S019; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S018: The Gaia Methodology for Agent-Oriented Analysis and Design (2000); EMAS-S019: MOISE+: towards a structural, functional, and deontic model for MAS organization (2002); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Organisational specification becomes an operational admission/reassignment question.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Check that a participant can fulfil a role, may exercise its powers and satisfies role compatibility/cardinality constraints before relying on it.

**Problem:** New role assignment, delegation or changed organisational membership.

**Mechanism:** Check that a participant can fulfil a role, may exercise its powers and satisfies role compatibility/cardinality constraints before relying on it.

**Trigger and context:** New role assignment, delegation or changed organisational membership.

**Preconditions:** Current capabilities, identity, compatibility rules and an authorised assignment process.

**Decision or consumer:** Role allocator and mission consumer

**Authority boundary:** Participant acceptance and organisational authorisation are distinct where required.

**Communication or interaction:** Role adoption, mission acceptance, normative changes and violations have identifiable communicative or environmental evidence. Specific coupling: Check that a participant can fulfil a role, may exercise its powers and satisfies role compatibility/cardinality constraints before relying on it.

**Observable criterion:** Role assignment does not leave an impossible mission or conflicting responsibility hidden in the organisation.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A direct eligibility check for a single stable role rather than a full role-management framework.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

<a id="emas-040"></a>
### EMAS-040 — Organisational revision with continuity constraints

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S050; EMAS-S019; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S050: Using the Moise+ for a Cooperative Framework of MAS Reorganisation (2004); EMAS-S019: MOISE+: towards a structural, functional, and deontic model for MAS organization (2002); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Identify a reason to reorganise, develop and select alternatives, implement through authorised actors and reconcile existing responsibilities.

**Development:** MOISE+ explicitly represents the organisation of reorganisation; continuity beyond its prototype is an analytical extension.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Identify a reason to reorganise, develop and select alternatives, implement through authorised actors and reconcile existing responsibilities.

**Problem:** Environment, task, capability or objective change makes the present organisation inadequate.

**Mechanism:** Identify a reason to reorganise, develop and select alternatives, implement through authorised actors and reconcile existing responsibilities.

**Trigger and context:** Environment, task, capability or objective change makes the present organisation inadequate.

**Preconditions:** Change authority, assessment criterion, current obligation state and an implementation path.

**Decision or consumer:** Organisation participants and authorised change decision maker

**Authority boundary:** Roles for proposing and selecting change do not create external authority to change others’ rights.

**Communication or interaction:** Role adoption, mission acceptance, normative changes and violations have identifiable communicative or environmental evidence. Specific coupling: Identify a reason to reorganise, develop and select alternatives, implement through authorised actors and reconcile existing responsibilities.

**Observable criterion:** Revision identifies what changed and who remains responsible for in-flight work.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Local reassignment or no structural change when the failure is not organisational.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Self-revision conceals self-authorisation; redesign cannot fix an impossible external goal.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S021; EMAS-S022; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S021; LIMITS: Constructed regulated water-market scenario, not evidence of real-world judicial enforcement. ### EMAS-042 — Role charts alone establish organisation

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S018; EMAS-S019; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S018: The Gaia Methodology for Agent-Oriented Analysis and Design (2000); EMAS-S019: MOISE+: towards a structural, functional, and deontic model for MAS organization (2002); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Preserve diagrams as useful representations when they change a decision or verify a constraint.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Reject role diagrams or natural-language job labels as evidence of functioning responsibilities, communications or enforcement.

**Problem:** A role chart is used as a substitute for demonstrated organisational operation.

**Mechanism:** Reject role diagrams or natural-language job labels as evidence of functioning responsibilities, communications or enforcement.

**Trigger and context:** A role chart is used as a substitute for demonstrated organisational operation.

**Preconditions:** Any useful chart must connect to task, authority, communication and consequence.

**Decision or consumer:** Organisation assessor

**Authority boundary:** A chart cannot assign rights that its author does not possess.

**Communication or interaction:** Role adoption, mission acceptance, normative changes and violations have identifiable communicative or environmental evidence. Specific coupling: Reject role diagrams or natural-language job labels as evidence of functioning responsibilities, communications or enforcement.

**Observable criterion:** Organisation is judged by enacted relations, not the presence of named roles.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A small executable responsibility arrangement with an actual consumer.

### EMAS-043 — Distributed constraint satisfaction under explicit transport and search assumptions

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S026; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S026: The Distributed Constraint Satisfaction Problem: Formalization and Algorithms (1998-09/1998-10); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Use local variable ownership, exchanged assignments/nogoods and a specified search/order discipline to find consistent assignments.

**Development:** Asynchronous algorithms relax global synchrony but not all communication or honesty assumptions.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Use local variable ownership, exchanged assignments/nogoods and a specified search/order discipline to find consistent assignments.

**Problem:** Constraints are genuinely distributed and parties can cooperate in a shared feasibility problem.

**Mechanism:** Use local variable ownership, exchanged assignments/nogoods and a specified search/order discipline to find consistent assignments.

**Trigger and context:** Constraints are genuinely distributed and parties can cooperate in a shared feasibility problem.

**Preconditions:** Finite domains, identified neighbours/agents and the algorithm’s reliable ordered-delivery assumptions.

**Decision or consumer:** Distributed solver participants

**Authority boundary:** Variable owners retain execution authority; solver output is a recommendation until validly enacted.

**Communication or interaction:** Constraint, utility, vote or argument messages have formally stated interpretations and transport assumptions. Specific coupling: Use local variable ownership, exchanged assignments/nogoods and a specified search/order discipline to find consistent assignments.

**Observable criterion:** The returned assignment satisfies the model’s constraints or the algorithm establishes no solution within its guarantee.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Central constraint solving or independent local checks when information and authority permit.

**Historical disposition:** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Failure modes:** Dropped messages, false constraints or changed variables invalidate completeness claims.

### EMAS-044 — Exact DCOP quality versus structural resource cost

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S027; EMAS-S028; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S027: ADOPT: Asynchronous Distributed Constraint Optimization with Quality Guarantees (2005); EMAS-S028: A Scalable Method for Multiagent Constraint Optimization (2005); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Choose search or dynamic programming using explicit quality guarantees and structural costs such as induced width, memory and communication.

**Mechanism:** Choose search or dynamic programming using explicit quality guarantees and structural costs such as induced width, memory and communication.

**Trigger and context:** The collective objective is correctly decomposed and a quality guarantee is worth its runtime cost.

### EMAS-045 — Privacy as a protocol-level disclosure property

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S029; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S029: A Privacy-Preserving Algorithm for DCOP (2014); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Define what others may infer from messages and results, then choose disclosure-limiting mechanisms under stated security assumptions.

**Development:** Privacy-preserving DCOP makes hidden inference and security costs explicit.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Define what others may infer from messages and results, then choose disclosure-limiting mechanisms under stated security assumptions.

**Problem:** Private values, constraints or participant information matter to the interaction.

**Mechanism:** Define what others may infer from messages and results, then choose disclosure-limiting mechanisms under stated security assumptions.

**Trigger and context:** Private values, constraints or participant information matter to the interaction.

**Preconditions:** A threat model, permitted leakage, cryptographic/behavioural assumptions and measurable overhead.

**Decision or consumer:** Participant and privacy assessor

**Authority boundary:** Information owners or legitimate controllers set disclosure rights; algorithms implement a bounded profile.

**Communication or interaction:** Constraint, utility, vote or argument messages have formally stated interpretations and transport assumptions. Specific coupling: Define what others may infer from messages and results, then choose disclosure-limiting mechanisms under stated security assumptions.

**Observable criterion:** Disclosure stays within the specified privacy definition, not merely local storage boundaries.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Data minimisation or authorised central handling when it offers the required protection more simply.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Output leakage, collusion or compromised participants violate stronger privacy claims than the protocol supports.

<a id="emas-046"></a>
### EMAS-046 — Argument acceptability relative to an explicit semantics

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S031; EMAS-S055; ACCOUNT: Dung (1995) supplies the abstract argumentation antecedent. Amgoud, Maudet and Parsons (2000), §2, explicitly identify that inspiration and add preferences; §§3–5 connect argument reasoning to inter-agent dialogue.; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: A documented import into a specific MAS dialogue model, not an inferred Dung-to-foundations-book genealogy and not a claim that all MAS argumentation shares the same semantics.

**Original form:** Construct an argument/attack framework and select a semantics appropriate to the decision; preserve absent or multiple extensions rather than inventing unanimity.

**Development:** A 1995 abstract reasoning model is explicitly adapted in 2000 to preference-sensitive two-party dialogue; 2002 methodological criticism separates public protocol conformance, private rationality and formal termination. The useful response is a conditional coupling of criterion and operating protocol, not the claim that every dialogue becomes truthful or terminating.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Construct an argument/attack framework and choose its acceptability semantics. Where agents must exchange reasons, couple that criterion to an explicit dialogue with assertion, challenge, support, acceptance and state-update rules appropriate to the selected model; do not treat permissible moves as a termination or truth guarantee.

**Problem:** Conflicting reasons must be compared without collapsing disagreement to a vote count.

**Mechanism:** Construct an argument/attack framework and choose its acceptability semantics. Where agents must exchange reasons, couple that criterion to an explicit dialogue with assertion, challenge, support, acceptance and state-update rules appropriate to the selected model; do not treat permissible moves as a termination or truth guarantee.

**Trigger and context:** Conflicting reasons must be compared without collapsing disagreement to a vote count.

**Preconditions:** Justified arguments/attacks and a stated grounded, preferred, stable or other semantics.; For the inspected 2000 dialogue branch: two participants, stratified knowledge, static agreed preference ordering, private argument-generation rules and public dialogue stores; relevance and useful stopping require additional behavioural conditions. These are not universal prerequisites of abstract argumentation.

**Decision or consumer:** Deliberative group and decision consumer

**Authority boundary:** Decision makers retain responsibility for enacting the selected conclusion.

**Communication or interaction:** An acceptability criterion needs a consumer. If reasons are communicated, distinguish argument support, challenge, acceptance, public store changes and private rationality assumptions. A dialogical commitment to a proposition does not by itself award a task or create an externally enforceable promise.

**Observable criterion:** The result is acceptable under the selected framework; no factual truth is inferred from acceptance alone.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Direct evidence checking or a simple explicit dispute may suffice when no structured attack analysis is needed.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Missing or mistaken premises/attacks and absent or multiple extensions can undermine a naive decision rule.; In the inspected dialogue model, legal repeated acceptances or challenges can continue indefinitely; the model assumes relevance and stopping behaviour rather than guaranteeing these from message syntax.; Opaque agents can satisfy observable dialogue rules without establishing the stipulated private reasoning or factual truth.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S055; EMAS-S056; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S055; LIMITS: The basic model is two-party, assumes static agreed preference strata and omits retraction. Selected definitions, protocol rules and stated proof results were inspected; predecessor proofs cited elsewhere were not independently checked. No comparative or deployment-effect evidence. Public dialogue commitments are not automatically promises about external task completion.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S056; LIMITS: Used for its authors’ methodological distinctions and criticism; origin claims are traced to the inspected EMAS-S055 and EMAS-S031 rather than accepting a review as proof of all cited work. No independent empirical replication. Author overlap with EMAS-S055 is explicit.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Acceptability is relative to the formal inputs and semantics; the quality of the evidence and authority to act remain separate. Repeated dialogue is not additional independent evidence.; ATTRIBUTION: The protocol’s relevance/stopping gap and public/private distinction are sourced; the cross-mechanism closure judgement is researcher analysis.

**Contrary evidence:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S055; EMAS-S056; ADJUDICATION: The stated dialogue can admit infinite legal exchanges without relevance/stopping assumptions; private conformance and factual input quality are not established by a public trace. This narrows transfer, not the abstract acceptability definitions.; NOT_A_REFUTATION_OF: A bounded theorem whose actual assumptions remain satisfied.

**Assumption sensitivity:** MATERIAL_ASSUMPTIONS: Justified arguments/attacks and a stated grounded, preferred, stable or other semantics.; For the inspected 2000 dialogue branch: two participants, stratified knowledge, static agreed preference ordering, private argument-generation rules and public dialogue stores; relevance and useful stopping require additional behavioural conditions. These are not universal prerequisites of abstract argumentation.; CHANGE_THAT_REOPENS_JUDGEMENT: What observation would change the argument graph rather than merely the voting weights?

**Unresolved questions:** What observation would change the argument graph rather than merely the voting weights?

**Property versus ceremony:** MINIMAL_ADEQUATE_FORM: Direct evidence checking or a simple explicit dispute may suffice when no structured attack analysis is needed.; ACTUAL_CONSUMER: Deliberative group and decision consumer; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT: Omit or retire when the stated trigger/dependency is absent and the observable postcondition is already secured by a cheaper adequate arrangement; preserve outstanding obligations during any transition.; FALSE_SUBSTITUTE: A representation or label without the stated postcondition and consumer does not establish this candidate.

This narrows transfer, not the abstract acceptability definitions.; TRANSFERABILITY: LABEL: CONDITIONAL; RATIONALE: Justified arguments/attacks and a stated grounded, preferred, stable or other semantics. **Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S025; EMAS-S023; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S025: Manipulation of Voting Schemes: A General Result (1973); EMAS-S023: Multiagent Systems: Algorithmic, Game-Theoretic, and Logical Foundations (2009; manuscript revision also ©2010); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Choose a voting rule with a stated preference domain, outcome space and manipulation criterion; disclose the guarantees sacrificed.

**Development:** Social-choice imports constrain MAS decision promises without prescribing one universally best rule.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Choose a voting rule with a stated preference domain, outcome space and manipulation criterion; disclose the guarantees sacrificed.

**Problem:** A group must choose among alternatives whose evaluations legitimately differ.

**Mechanism:** Choose a voting rule with a stated preference domain, outcome space and manipulation criterion; disclose the guarantees sacrificed.

**Trigger and context:** A group must choose among alternatives whose evaluations legitimately differ.

**Preconditions:** Electorate, alternatives, preference assumptions and a valid decision authority.

**Decision or consumer:** Collective decision organiser

**Authority boundary:** A computational ballot does not establish legitimate franchise or external decision rights.

**Communication or interaction:** Constraint, utility, vote or argument messages have formally stated interpretations and transport assumptions. Specific coupling: Choose a voting rule with a stated preference domain, outcome space and manipulation criterion; disclose the guarantees sacrificed.

**Observable criterion:** The selected rule’s fairness and incentive claims match its domain rather than an impossible universal ideal.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** An already authorised decision maker or direct factual test when collective preference aggregation is unnecessary.

**Historical disposition:** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

### EMAS-048 — Evidence provenance and dependence-aware aggregation

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S013; EMAS-S043; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S013: Agent Communication Languages: Rethinking the Principles (1998-12); EMAS-S043: HAC-ER: A Disaster Response System based on Human-Agent Collectives (2016); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Public evidence semantics and provenance-aware human-agent work are extended analytically to shared-model collectives.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Trace repeated claims to originating observations, data and models; assess corroboration at the dependency level rather than counting speakers.

**Problem:** Multiple agents or human reports appear to corroborate a factual conclusion.

**Mechanism:** Trace repeated claims to originating observations, data and models; assess corroboration at the dependency level rather than counting speakers.

**Trigger and context:** Multiple agents or human reports appear to corroborate a factual conclusion.

**Preconditions:** Provenance sufficient to distinguish shared sources from genuinely distinct observations.

**Decision or consumer:** Decision maker relying on corroboration

**Authority boundary:** Aggregators can assess evidence but cannot manufacture access to hidden training provenance.

**Communication or interaction:** Constraint, utility, vote or argument messages have formally stated interpretations and transport assumptions. Specific coupling: Trace repeated claims to originating observations, data and models; assess corroboration at the dependency level rather than counting speakers.

**Observable criterion:** Agreement is labelled with its evidence dependence; repeated copies do not inflate an independence claim.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Inspect the primary observation directly when additional aggregation adds no independent information.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Unknown shared training or hidden data reuse prevents reliable independence estimates.

Bootstrap uncertainty and imperfect token controls matter. The broad title is not accepted as a universal theorem.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Unknown shared training or hidden data reuse prevents reliable independence estimates.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.

<a id="emas-050"></a>
### EMAS-050 — Non-stationarity-sensitive learning evaluation

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S032; EMAS-S034; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S032: Multi-Agent Actor-Critic for Mixed Cooperative–Competitive Environments (2017); EMAS-S034: “Other-Play” for Zero-Shot Coordination (2020); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Multi-agent learning practice separates training stability from test-time strategic or partner robustness.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Evaluate policies against changing partners and update rules rather than treating all other agents as a fixed environment.

**Problem:** Other participants learn, adapt or enter after policy training.

**Mechanism:** Evaluate policies against changing partners and update rules rather than treating all other agents as a fixed environment.

**Trigger and context:** Other participants learn, adapt or enter after policy training.

**Preconditions:** Declared update schedules, partner populations and evaluation horizon.

**Decision or consumer:** Learning-system evaluator

**Authority boundary:** Learning actors may change policies only within their operational permission.

**Communication or interaction:** Learned signalling or text communication must be tested for partner-compatible meaning and overhead, not assumed from fluency. Specific coupling: Evaluate policies against changing partners and update rules rather than treating all other agents as a fixed environment.

**Observable criterion:** Reported policy quality identifies whether partners were fixed, jointly trained or adapting.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Stationary single-agent evaluation when other behaviour is genuinely fixed and that condition is maintained.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** A policy improves against an obsolete partner or destabilises mutual adaptation.

### EMAS-051 — Centralised training with decentralised execution information contract

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S032; EMAS-S033; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S032: Multi-Agent Actor-Critic for Mixed Cooperative–Competitive Environments (2017); EMAS-S033: QMIX: Monotonic Value Function Factorisation for Deep Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (2018); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Use joint information during training while ensuring each deployed policy depends only on observations available to its executing agent.

**Development:** CTDE is a useful architecture family, not a proof of partner generalisation or global optimality.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Use joint information during training while ensuring each deployed policy depends only on observations available to its executing agent.

**Problem:** Joint training can address credit or non-stationarity while deployment information is local.

**Mechanism:** Use joint information during training while ensuring each deployed policy depends only on observations available to its executing agent.

**Trigger and context:** Joint training can address credit or non-stationarity while deployment information is local.

**Preconditions:** Training access to joint state/actions and a verified execution-time observation boundary.

**Decision or consumer:** Learning architect

**Authority boundary:** Training access does not confer deployment access or authority.

**Communication or interaction:** Learned signalling or text communication must be tested for partner-compatible meaning and overhead, not assumed from fluency. Specific coupling: Use joint information during training while ensuring each deployed policy depends only on observations available to its executing agent.

**Observable criterion:** No evaluation or deployment action relies on training-only privileged information.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Independent learning or central execution if those match the actual information and authority constraints.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Information leakage inflates performance; central critic assumptions fail with changed partners.

### EMAS-052 — Collective credit assignment and reward alignment

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S032; EMAS-S033; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S032: Multi-Agent Actor-Critic for Mixed Cooperative–Competitive Environments (2017); EMAS-S033: QMIX: Monotonic Value Function Factorisation for Deep Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (2018); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Choose team or local rewards and credit signals in relation to the intended joint objective; inspect who benefits and what behaviours are rewarded.

**Development:** Credit mechanisms address learning difficulty while leaving objective validity and external legitimacy separate.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Choose team or local rewards and credit signals in relation to the intended joint objective; inspect who benefits and what behaviours are rewarded.

**Problem:** Useful outcomes depend on contributions whose individual effects are hard to distinguish.

**Mechanism:** Choose team or local rewards and credit signals in relation to the intended joint objective; inspect who benefits and what behaviours are rewarded.

**Trigger and context:** Useful outcomes depend on contributions whose individual effects are hard to distinguish.

**Preconditions:** An operationally meaningful reward, evaluable contributions and known coupling to collective outcomes.

**Decision or consumer:** Learning designer and outcome owner

**Authority boundary:** Objective designers cannot equate their chosen reward with participants’ full interests.

**Communication or interaction:** Learned signalling or text communication must be tested for partner-compatible meaning and overhead, not assumed from fluency. Specific coupling: Choose team or local rewards and credit signals in relation to the intended joint objective; inspect who benefits and what behaviours are rewarded.

**Observable criterion:** Improved learning signals support the declared objective within the tested model, not an unmeasured social good.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A directly observed task outcome or deterministic allocation when learning a credit rule is unnecessary.

**Historical disposition:** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Failure modes:** Reward hacking, free riding and locally rational extraction can harm the collective.

### EMAS-053 — Local-to-joint value factorisation under representational restrictions

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S033; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S033: QMIX: Monotonic Value Function Factorisation for Deep Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (2018); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Use monotonic value mixing only where its representational restriction is acceptable; distinguish decentralised argmax consistency from optimal policy learning.

**Development:** QMIX contributes a precise restricted correspondence, not a generic local-to-global optimality theorem.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Use monotonic value mixing only where its representational restriction is acceptable; distinguish decentralised argmax consistency from optimal policy learning.

**Problem:** A cooperative joint action-value model needs decentralised greedy action selection.

**Mechanism:** Use monotonic value mixing only where its representational restriction is acceptable; distinguish decentralised argmax consistency from optimal policy learning.

**Trigger and context:** A cooperative joint action-value model needs decentralised greedy action selection.

**Preconditions:** Monotonic mixing and suitable local utility representation; training assumptions remain separate.

**Decision or consumer:** MARL architect

**Authority boundary:** Agent executors choose permitted local actions; utility factorisation grants no new control.

**Communication or interaction:** Learned signalling or text communication must be tested for partner-compatible meaning and overhead, not assumed from fluency. Specific coupling: Use monotonic value mixing only where its representational restriction is acceptable; distinguish decentralised argmax consistency from optimal policy learning.

**Observable criterion:** Local greedy choices match the argmax of the represented joint value function under the condition.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Central action selection or another representation when non-monotonic interactions matter.

**Historical disposition:** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Failure modes:** The best policy requires a non-monotonic value structure; training fails even though the architecture is consistent.

**Original form:** Test separately trained, unfamiliar or differently adapted partners; use symmetry-aware methods only when their task symmetries are justified.

**Mechanism:** Test separately trained, unfamiliar or differently adapted partners; use symmetry-aware methods only when their task symmetries are justified.

### EMAS-055 — Emergent conventions are evaluated, not presumed legitimate norms

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S023; EMAS-S039; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S023: Multiagent Systems: Algorithmic, Game-Theoretic, and Logical Foundations (2009; manuscript revision also ©2010); EMAS-S039: The Role of Social Learning and Collective Norm Formation in Fostering Cooperation in LLM Multi-Agent Systems (2026-05); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Mechanism:** Distinguish recurrent behaviour, a stabilised convention, explicit obligations and externally legitimate institutional rules; evaluate each against its own criteria.

**Trigger and context:** A learned or LLM collective is said to have developed norms or cooperation.

Specific coupling: Distinguish recurrent behaviour, a stabilised convention, explicit obligations and externally legitimate institutional rules; evaluate each against its own criteria.

**Observable criterion:** An emergent pattern is not called desirable solely because it persists or participants endorse it.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Describe the observed regularity without adding institutional machinery or moral conclusions.

**Failure modes:** Exploitative equilibria, collusion and resource depletion can stabilise.

### EMAS-058 — Failure, deception and common-mode threat model

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S047; EMAS-S051; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S047: Knowledge and Common Knowledge in a Distributed Environment (1990); EMAS-S051: Byzantine Fault Tolerance in Distributed Constraint Optimization Problems (2026-05); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Explicit fault-tolerant DCOP demonstrates model-specific adaptation rather than blanket imported terminology.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Specify crash, delay, false report, strategic deviation, arbitrary corruption and common dependencies separately before choosing robustness mechanisms.

**Problem:** The collective relies on agents, transport or data that may fail or misbehave.

**Mechanism:** Specify crash, delay, false report, strategic deviation, arbitrary corruption and common dependencies separately before choosing robustness mechanisms.

**Trigger and context:** The collective relies on agents, transport or data that may fail or misbehave.

**Preconditions:** A stated invariant, fault scope, trusted components and observation limits.

**Decision or consumer:** Reliability and security analyst

**Authority boundary:** Designers may restrict trusted computation but cannot assume honest external actors by decree.

**Communication or interaction:** Failure notices, state reconciliation and retry messages require correlation; network partitions do not create new authority. Specific coupling: Specify crash, delay, false report, strategic deviation, arbitrary corruption and common dependencies separately before choosing robustness mechanisms.

**Observable criterion:** Each robustness claim names the failures it covers and the common failures it does not.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Ordinary monitored recovery for a low-stakes cooperative system; no Byzantine machinery without that threat.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Multiple role names conceal one model or one compromised information source.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S049; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S049; LIMITS: Tests isolate direct-trust components of larger frameworks, with two agents/400 interactions and specified attack cycles. ### EMAS-059 — Recovery from abandoned obligations

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S009; EMAS-S044; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S009: Towards Flexible Teamwork (1997); EMAS-S044: Multiagent Commitment Alignment (2009); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Team repair and commitment alignment jointly motivate this analytical recovery pattern; no general exactly-once theorem is claimed.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Detect missing progress, reconcile what work may already have occurred, and reassign, compensate or terminate within the obligation’s authority limits.

**Problem:** A participant departs or a contribution is unavailable while others depend on it.

**Mechanism:** Detect missing progress, reconcile what work may already have occurred, and reassign, compensate or terminate within the obligation’s authority limits.

**Trigger and context:** A participant departs or a contribution is unavailable while others depend on it.

**Preconditions:** Task/commitment identity, a failure-observation policy and bounded recovery authority.

**Decision or consumer:** Affected counterparty and recovery coordinator

**Authority boundary:** Recovery actor may change responsibility only under an agreed rule or external authorisation.

**Communication or interaction:** Failure notices, state reconciliation and retry messages require correlation; network partitions do not create new authority. Specific coupling: Detect missing progress, reconcile what work may already have occurred, and reassign, compensate or terminate within the obligation’s authority limits.

**Observable criterion:** No obligation disappears merely because an agent process is gone; duplicate-effect uncertainty is handled explicitly.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A direct verified retry or explicit task failure when no overlapping effect is possible.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** A slow agent resumes after reassignment; repeated recovery loops make no progress.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S047; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S047; LIMITS: Model-dependent impossibility; weaker coordination objectives or different delivery assumptions must be analysed separately. <a id="emas-060"></a>
### EMAS-060 — Risk-limited trust and reputation

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S049; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S049: Towards Con-resistant Trust Models for Distributed Agent Systems (2009); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Use reputation as fallible evidence for a bounded interaction; consider repeated defection, recovery dynamics and the magnitude of the next exposure.

**Development:** Con-resistant updates retain longer adverse history but cannot prevent a first attack.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Use reputation as fallible evidence for a bounded interaction; consider repeated defection, recovery dynamics and the magnitude of the next exposure.

**Problem:** Past conduct informs selection or delegation to a partially trusted participant.

**Mechanism:** Use reputation as fallible evidence for a bounded interaction; consider repeated defection, recovery dynamics and the magnitude of the next exposure.

**Trigger and context:** Past conduct informs selection or delegation to a partially trusted participant.

**Preconditions:** Identity continuity, relevant observations and an explicit model of noise and strategic manipulation.

**Decision or consumer:** Delegating or trading participant

**Authority boundary:** A reputation evaluator may bound its own exposure, not assign universal moral status to a participant.

**Communication or interaction:** Failure notices, state reconciliation and retry messages require correlation; network partitions do not create new authority. Specific coupling: Use reputation as fallible evidence for a bounded interaction; consider repeated defection, recovery dynamics and the magnitude of the next exposure.

**Observable criterion:** Trust scores do not substitute for evidence of the current high-consequence action.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Direct verification, a small reversible trial or refusal of high-risk delegation when more reliable.

**Historical disposition:** USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED

**Failure modes:** Con strategies rebuild reputation before costly defection; strict penalties punish noisy honest actors.

### EMAS-061 — Collective progress separate from local compliance

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S006; EMAS-S016; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S006: Using Partial Global Plans to Coordinate Distributed Problem Solvers (1987); EMAS-S016: Semantics and Verification of Information-Based Protocols (2012); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Formal completion possibility is coupled analytically to operational monitoring without claiming the two definitions are identical.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Observe useful collective progress and terminal outcomes in addition to legal local transitions or message exchange.

**Problem:** A system can remain locally compliant while waiting, repeating plans or deferring work indefinitely.

**Mechanism:** Observe useful collective progress and terminal outcomes in addition to legal local transitions or message exchange.

**Trigger and context:** A system can remain locally compliant while waiting, repeating plans or deferring work indefinitely.

**Preconditions:** A useful-progress definition, waiting assumptions and a consumer empowered to respond.

**Decision or consumer:** Outcome owner

**Authority boundary:** Progress observers can request repair; goal changes or termination follow the relevant authority.

**Communication or interaction:** Failure notices, state reconciliation and retry messages require correlation; network partitions do not create new authority. Specific coupling: Observe useful collective progress and terminal outcomes in addition to legal local transitions or message exchange.

**Observable criterion:** The system can distinguish active useful work from endless compliant coordination.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** One outcome deadline or progress observation may suffice for a simple bounded task.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Metrics count messages or proposals as progress; true environment work remains undone.

### EMAS-062 — Coordination-layer failure and fallback

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S006; EMAS-S051; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S006: Using Partial Global Plans to Coordinate Distributed Problem Solvers (1987); EMAS-S051: Byzantine Fault Tolerance in Distributed Constraint Optimization Problems (2026-05); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Central versus distributed coordination and fault-specific replication remain selectable risk/cost alternatives.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Identify shared coordinators, message services and monitors; provide a justified degraded mode, alternative coordination path or explicit safe suspension.

**Problem:** A coordination dependency can disable otherwise capable participants.

**Mechanism:** Identify shared coordinators, message services and monitors; provide a justified degraded mode, alternative coordination path or explicit safe suspension.

**Trigger and context:** A coordination dependency can disable otherwise capable participants.

**Preconditions:** Known dependency graph, fallback rights and reconciliation of in-flight actions.

**Decision or consumer:** System operator

**Authority boundary:** Fallback is not permission to ignore external constraints or invent a new owner.

**Communication or interaction:** Failure notices, state reconciliation and retry messages require correlation; network partitions do not create new authority. Specific coupling: Identify shared coordinators, message services and monitors; provide a justified degraded mode, alternative coordination path or explicit safe suspension.

**Observable criterion:** Failure of coordination causes the declared bounded degradation rather than unrecognised conflicting action.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Accept a single coordination service with sufficient recovery when redundancy costs more than its risk reduction.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Fallback bypasses safety constraints or creates split responsibility; replicas share the same defect.

### EMAS-063 — Open membership and version-compatible admission

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S019; EMAS-S044; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S019: MOISE+: towards a structural, functional, and deontic model for MAS organization (2002); EMAS-S044: Multiagent Commitment Alignment (2009); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Development:** Organisational reconfiguration and protocol alignment are coupled as an analytical admission rule, not a universal runtime standard.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** When membership or versions change, establish compatible role, protocol and obligation assumptions and identify responsibility for in-flight interactions.

**Problem:** An agent joins, leaves or changes its implementation/ontology during ongoing activity.

**Mechanism:** When membership or versions change, establish compatible role, protocol and obligation assumptions and identify responsibility for in-flight interactions.

**Trigger and context:** An agent joins, leaves or changes its implementation/ontology during ongoing activity.

**Preconditions:** Identities, declared versions/capabilities and a decision about outstanding commitments.

**Decision or consumer:** Organisation and protocol participants

**Authority boundary:** Admission controller or participants act only within their institution’s membership rights.

Specific coupling: When membership or versions change, establish compatible role, protocol and obligation assumptions and identify responsibility for in-flight interactions.

**Observable criterion:** New participation does not silently invalidate the meanings or guarantees relied upon by existing parties.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Closed fixed membership with explicit restart boundaries when dynamic admission is unnecessary.

### EMAS-065 — Matched-budget and credible simpler baselines

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S023; EMAS-S037; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S023: Multiagent Systems: Algorithmic, Game-Theoretic, and Logical Foundations (2009; manuscript revision also ©2010); EMAS-S037: Single-Agent LLMs Outperform Multi-Agent Systems on Multi-Hop Reasoning Under Equal Thinking Token Budgets (2026-04-02); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Positive debate results are tested against cost-normalised work; negative results are also limited to their actual settings.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Compare alternatives on the same task and comparable tools, information, compute and cost; disclose budgets that cannot genuinely be matched.

**Problem:** Claiming a multi-agent performance or efficiency advantage.

**Mechanism:** Compare alternatives on the same task and comparable tools, information, compute and cost; disclose budgets that cannot genuinely be matched.

**Trigger and context:** Claiming a multi-agent performance or efficiency advantage.

**Preconditions:** Valid task measures, comparable capability/access and clearly accounted training versus inference costs.

**Decision or consumer:** Researcher and system purchaser

**Authority boundary:** Evaluator controls the comparison, not the real-world acceptance criterion.

**Communication or interaction:** Trace review separates valid messages, consequential understanding and actual progress; shared prompts and models create evidence dependence. Specific coupling: Compare alternatives on the same task and comparable tools, information, compute and cost; disclose budgets that cannot genuinely be matched.

**Observable criterion:** The reported advantage survives the stated comparison or is narrowed when it does not.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A small discriminating baseline experiment before an expensive broad comparison.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Extra reasoning tokens, training data or tool access masquerade as coordination benefit.

### EMAS-068 — Mixed human-agent participation and decision boundaries

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S022; EMAS-S043; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S022: Multi-Agent Systems: Technical & Ethical Challenges of Functioning in a Mixed Group (2022); EMAS-S043: HAC-ER: A Disaster Response System based on Human-Agent Collectives (2016); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Mixed-initiative field programmes and ethical analysis preserve human participation without assuming human presence guarantees safety.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Make human decisions, agent recommendations, vetoes, explanations and control transfers explicit; evaluate whether people can actually use the information and retain the intended authority.

**Problem:** People work with agents or bear consequences of their coordination.

**Mechanism:** Make human decisions, agent recommendations, vetoes, explanations and control transfers explicit; evaluate whether people can actually use the information and retain the intended authority.

**Trigger and context:** People work with agents or bear consequences of their coordination.

**Preconditions:** Participant roles, usable interfaces, training and feasible communication with authorised humans.

**Decision or consumer:** Affected participant and operational decision maker

**Authority boundary:** External human and institutional rights are not created by the interface.

**Communication or interaction:** Trace review separates valid messages, consequential understanding and actual progress; shared prompts and models create evidence dependence. Specific coupling: Make human decisions, agent recommendations, vetoes, explanations and control transfers explicit; evaluate whether people can actually use the information and retain the intended authority.

**Observable criterion:** Human involvement changes real choices or accountability rather than serving as a ceremonial approval label.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A human-controlled decision aid can be preferable to automated group authority.

### EMAS-069 — End-to-end collective consequence evaluation

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S043; EMAS-S038; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S043: HAC-ER: A Disaster Response System based on Human-Agent Collectives (2016); EMAS-S038: Benchmarking Open-Ended Multi-Agent Coordination in Language Agents (2026-06-06); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Development:** Coordination-specific benchmarks and task-verification failures motivate a separate collective outcome consumer.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Evaluate the final collective consequence, including timing, quality and harms, rather than inferring it from completed subtasks or successful conversations.

**Problem:** All local tasks may complete while their combination fails the overall purpose.

**Mechanism:** Evaluate the final collective consequence, including timing, quality and harms, rather than inferring it from completed subtasks or successful conversations.

**Trigger and context:** All local tasks may complete while their combination fails the overall purpose.

**Preconditions:** A declared collective objective and an observation method independent of local self-certification where required.

**Decision or consumer:** Collective task recipient

**Authority boundary:** Outcome assessor may reject a result without automatically authorising new actions.

**Communication or interaction:** Trace review separates valid messages, consequential understanding and actual progress; shared prompts and models create evidence dependence. Specific coupling: Evaluate the final collective consequence, including timing, quality and harms, rather than inferring it from completed subtasks or successful conversations.

**Observable criterion:** Local completion and collective success can be recorded differently.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** One well-defined final acceptance observation when it adequately captures the consequence.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Individually correct outputs do not fit together; a judge shares the same misunderstanding as the agents.

### EMAS-070 — Deployment, adoption and causal benefit are separate evidence

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S002; EMAS-S042; EMAS-S043; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S002: International Foundation for Autonomous Agents and Multiagent Systems: About (undated; accessed 2026-09-06); EMAS-S042: ARMOR Security for Los Angeles International Airport (2008); EMAS-S043: HAC-ER: A Disaster Response System based on Human-Agent Collectives (2016); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.

**Development:** Field reports remain valuable but are partitioned from comparative effectiveness.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Report whether evidence establishes implementation, use, measured comparison or causal effect; do not promote one evidence role into another.

**Problem:** A deployed demonstration or popular framework is cited as proof of effectiveness.

**Mechanism:** Report whether evidence establishes implementation, use, measured comparison or causal effect; do not promote one evidence role into another.

**Trigger and context:** A deployed demonstration or popular framework is cited as proof of effectiveness.

**Preconditions:** Exact programme, setting, comparator and outcome identification.

**Decision or consumer:** Research consumer

**Authority boundary:** Researchers assess evidence; adopters and participants determine acceptable use.

**Communication or interaction:** Trace review separates valid messages, consequential understanding and actual progress; shared prompts and models create evidence dependence. **Problem:** Initial design, persistent coordination cost or changed task/dependency structure.

**Mechanism:** Select the smallest compatible mechanism set that addresses the real interdependence, then omit or retire machinery when its dependency, risk or consumer disappears.

<a id="emas-075"></a>
### EMAS-075 — Fault-model-specific replicated optimisation

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S051; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S051: Byzantine Fault Tolerance in Distributed Constraint Optimization Problems (2026-05); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Replicate the required factor/variable computations and verify sequence-correlated deterministic messages under the FT-DCOP replica assumptions, preserving fault-free Max-Sum behaviour.

**Development:** 2026 FT-DCOP explicitly extends the model; it is not a universal Byzantine recipe or an independence theorem.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Replicate the required factor/variable computations and verify sequence-correlated deterministic messages under the FT-DCOP replica assumptions, preserving fault-free Max-Sum behaviour.

**Problem:** Arbitrary faulty DCOP participants are in scope and the required knowledge/replica coverage is feasible.

**Mechanism:** Replicate the required factor/variable computations and verify sequence-correlated deterministic messages under the FT-DCOP replica assumptions, preserving fault-free Max-Sum behaviour.

**Trigger and context:** Arbitrary faulty DCOP participants are in scope and the required knowledge/replica coverage is feasible.

**Preconditions:** For the analysed construction, at least 2k+1 replicas per relevant group, at most k faulty, correct function knowledge and the specified synchronous message-passing and round discipline.

**Decision or consumer:** DCOP reliability designer

**Authority boundary:** Replicated computations do not authorise actions outside the optimisation participants’ rights.

**Communication or interaction:** Failure notices, state reconciliation and retry messages require correlation; network partitions do not create new authority. Specific coupling: Replicate the required factor/variable computations and verify sequence-correlated deterministic messages under the FT-DCOP replica assumptions, preserving fault-free Max-Sum behaviour.

**Observable criterion:** Correct replicas reproduce the fault-free underlying algorithm’s messages under the model; approximation quality is not strengthened into exact optimality.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Crash recovery or unreplicated optimisation when Byzantine behaviour is out of scope and lower-cost controls suffice.

**Historical disposition:** DOMAIN_SPECIFIC

**Failure modes:** Common corrupt utilities, too many faulty replicas or incompatible transport defeat the guarantee; communication overhead grows quadratically with k per original message.

<a id="emas-076"></a>
### EMAS-076 — Norm-aware plan augmentation and deliberation

**Historical origin:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S052; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S052: Engineering Norm-aware BDI Agents (2026-05); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.

**Original form:** Translate applicable norms into goal-plan choices that distinguish added compliance steps, violation consequences and non-applicability, then integrate them with the agent’s plan selection.

**Development:** 2026 norm-aware BDI work demonstrates a running-example implementation in an existing platform; general overhead and dynamic revision remain research questions.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** Translate applicable norms into goal-plan choices that distinguish added compliance steps, violation consequences and non-applicability, then integrate them with the agent’s plan selection.

**Problem:** An agent must reason about a norm rather than merely have forbidden actions blocked externally.

**Mechanism:** Translate applicable norms into goal-plan choices that distinguish added compliance steps, violation consequences and non-applicability, then integrate them with the agent’s plan selection.

**Trigger and context:** An agent must reason about a norm rather than merely have forbidden actions blocked externally.

**Preconditions:** Known norm conditions, available compliance actions and a justified valuation/plan-selection procedure.

**Decision or consumer:** Agent programmer and normative interaction designer

**Authority boundary:** Ability to represent violation is descriptive decision support, not permission to violate real obligations.

**Communication or interaction:** Role adoption, mission acceptance, normative changes and violations have identifiable communicative or environmental evidence. Specific coupling: Translate applicable norms into goal-plan choices that distinguish added compliance steps, violation consequences and non-applicability, then integrate them with the agent’s plan selection.

**Observable criterion:** The selected plan reflects norm-related steps and consequences in the implementation’s represented choices.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A fixed compliant plan or direct enforcement when deliberating about alternatives adds no necessary flexibility.

**Historical disposition:** DOMAIN_SPECIFIC

**Failure modes:** Incorrect valuations normalise harmful violations; norm awareness is mistaken for compliance or legitimacy.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S020; EMAS-S021; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S020; LIMITS: The model assumes actions observable and effects determined by the norm artefact. A simulated fine does not establish legal power over real people. Criticism of other frameworks is scoped to versions discussed.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S021; LIMITS: Constructed regulated water-market scenario, not evidence of real-world judicial enforcement. The 2010 event and 2011 publication dates are distinct. PDF visual-rendering attempt failed; text sections were readable.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Incorrect valuations normalise harmful violations; norm awareness is mistaken for compliance or legitimacy.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.
